### Run pytest with conftest example

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/writing_plugins.rst

Commands to run the tests from the conftest.py example. The first command with test_flat.py will not show the setup message, the second with a/test_sub.py will show it.

```bash
pytest test_flat.py --capture=no  # will not show "setting up"
pytest a/test_sub.py --capture=no  # will show "setting up"
```

--------------------------------

### Set up virtual environment and run pytest directly

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/CONTRIBUTING.rst

Set up a virtual environment and install the project with the dev dependency group (requires pip 25.1+). Alternatively, use uv sync. After setup, run pytest normally.

```bash
$ python3 -m venv .venv
$ source .venv/bin/activate  # Linux
$ .venv/Scripts/activate.bat  # Windows
$ pip install -e . --group dev
```

```bash
$ uv sync --group dev
```

```bash
$ pytest testing/test_config.py
```

### Example tests_to_run.txt file

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/usage.rst

Example content for the tests_to_run.txt file used with the '@' prefix. It contains one entry per line.

```text
tests/test_file.py
tests/test_mod.py::test_func[x1,y2]
tests/test_mod.py::TestClass
-m slow
```

### Debugging with print statements in test_module.py

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/capture-stdout-stderr.rst

Example test module using print statements in setup_function to demonstrate that captured output is shown only for failing tests. The setup prints the function name, and test_func2 fails, revealing its captured output.

```python
# content of test_module.py


def setup_function(function):
    print("setting up", function)


def test_func1():
    assert True


def test_func2():
    assert False
```

### setup.cfg configuration example

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/reference/customize.rst

Example of a setup.cfg configuration file with a [tool:pytest] section. Usage of setup.cfg is not recommended unless for very simple use cases, as .cfg files use a different parser than pytest.ini and tox.ini.

```ini
# setup.cfg
[tool:pytest]
minversion = 6.0
addopts = -ra -q
testpaths =
    tests
    integration
```

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/reference/customize.rst

```toml
# pytest.toml or .pytest.toml
[pytest]
minversion = "9.0"
addopts = ["-ra", "-q"]
testpaths = [
    "tests",
    "integration",
]
```

### Copy example files with pytester.copy_example and run pytest

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/writing_plugins.rst

Shows how to copy example files into pytester's isolated environment. Requires setting pytester_example_dir in the configuration file (pytest.toml) to tell pytest where to look for examples. The test file uses pytester.copy_example() and runs pytest with -k test_example. The output shows the expected pytest run result with 2 passed.

```toml
# content of pytest.toml
[pytest]
pytester_example_dir = "."
```

```python
# content of test_example.py


def test_plugin(pytester):
    pytester.copy_example("test_example.py")
    pytester.runpytest("-k", "test_example")


def test_example():
    pass
```

```pytest
$ pytest
=========================== test session starts ============================
platform linux -- Python 3.x.y, pytest-9.x.y, pluggy-1.x.y
rootdir: /home/sweet/project
configfile: pytest.toml
collected 2 items

test_example.py ..                                                   [100%]

============================ 2 passed in 0.12s =============================
```

### Nose-style setup method (deprecated)

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/deprecations.rst

Plain setup and teardown methods are part of nose support, not pytest native. This usage is deprecated.

```python
class Test:
    def setup(self):
        self.resource = make_resource()
```

```bash
                  # 'python setup.py develop' and 'conda develop'

```

### Example plugin providing hello fixture

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/writing_plugins.rst

This plugin adds a --name command line option and provides a 'hello' fixture that returns a function. The function returns 'Hello World!' by default or 'Hello {value}!' when a value is supplied.

```python
import pytest


def pytest_addoption(parser):
    group = parser.getgroup("helloworld")
    group.addoption(
        "--name",
        action="store",
        dest="name",
        default="World",
        help='Default "name" for hello().',
    )


@pytest.fixture
def hello(request):
    name = request.config.getoption("name")

    def _hello(name=None):
        if not name:
            name = request.config.getoption("name")
        return f"Hello {name}!"

    return _hello
```

### Yield fixtures for teardown with emaillib example

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/fixtures.rst

Shows a complete example of using yield fixtures for teardown. The `emaillib.py` module defines the email classes, and `test_emaillib.py` uses fixtures that yield and then perform cleanup after the test. The command runs the test and shows the expected output.

```python
# content of emaillib.py
class MailAdminClient:
    def create_user(self):
        return MailUser()

    def delete_user(self, user):
        # do some cleanup
        pass


class MailUser:
    def __init__(self):
        self.inbox = []

    def send_email(self, email, other):
        other.inbox.append(email)

    def clear_mailbox(self):
        self.inbox.clear()


class Email:
    def __init__(self, subject, body):
        self.subject = subject
        self.body = body
```

```python
# content of test_emaillib.py
from emaillib import Email, MailAdminClient

import pytest


@pytest.fixture
def mail_admin():
    return MailAdminClient()


@pytest.fixture
def sending_user(mail_admin):
    user = mail_admin.create_user()
    yield user
    mail_admin.delete_user(user)


@pytest.fixture
def receiving_user(mail_admin):
    user = mail_admin.create_user()
    yield user
    user.clear_mailbox()
    mail_admin.delete_user(user)


def test_email_received(sending_user, receiving_user):
    email = Email(subject="Hey!", body="How's it going?")
    sending_user.send_email(email, receiving_user)
    assert email in receiving_user.inbox
```

```bash
$ pytest -q test_emaillib.py
.                                                                    [100%]
1 passed in 0.12s
```

### Inspect caplog records for setup and call stages in a fixture

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/logging.rst

Use caplog.get_records(when) inside a fixture teardown to check for warnings during the 'setup' and 'call' stages. Fails the test if any warning-level messages are found.

```python
@pytest.fixture
def window(caplog):
    window = create_window()
    yield window
    for when in ("setup", "call"):
        messages = [
            x.message for x in caplog.get_records(when) if x.levelno == logging.WARNING
        ]
        if messages:
            pytest.fail(f"warning messages encountered during testing: {messages}")
```

--------------------------------

### Hook ordering example with tryfirst, trylast, and wrapper

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/writing_hook_functions.rst

Shows three plugins implementing the same hook with different ordering options. The wrapper executes before tryfirst, and its finally block runs after all non-wrappers.

```python
# Plugin 1
@pytest.hookimpl(tryfirst=True)
def pytest_collection_modifyitems(items):
    # will execute as early as possible
    ...


# Plugin 2
@pytest.hookimpl(trylast=True)
def pytest_collection_modifyitems(items):
    # will execute as late as possible
    ...


# Plugin 3
@pytest.hookimpl(wrapper=True)
def pytest_collection_modifyitems(items):
    # will execute even before the tryfirst one above!
    try:
        return (yield)
    finally:
        # will execute after all non-wrappers executed
        ...
```

### Implement pytest_runtest_setup hook in conftest.py

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/writing_plugins.rst

This example shows a conftest.py file that implements the pytest_runtest_setup hook to print a message for each test in the 'a' subdirectory. It also includes the test files. Running 'pytest test_flat.py --capture=no' will not show the message, while running 'pytest a/test_sub.py --capture=no' will show "setting up".

```python
a/conftest.py:
    def pytest_runtest_setup(item):
        # called for running each test in 'a' directory
        print("setting up", item)

a/test_sub.py:
    def test_sub():
        pass

test_flat.py:
    def test_flat():
        pass
```

### Nose @with_setup decorator example

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/deprecations.rst

Original nose-style code using the @with_setup decorator to set up and tear down resources for a test. This is the code that needs to be ported to a supported pytest style.

```python
from nose.tools import with_setup


def setup_some_resource(): ...


def teardown_some_resource(): ...


@with_setup(setup_some_resource, teardown_some_resource)
def test_foo(): ...
```

### Run multiple asserts with class-scoped autouse fixture

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/fixtures.rst

Shows a class-scoped fixture setup for end-to-end login tests. The autouse 'login' fixture acts as the setup step, and each test method requests only the fixtures it needs. Assumes base_url and admin_credentials fixtures exist elsewhere.

```python
# contents of tests/end_to_end/test_login.py
from uuid import uuid4
from urllib.parse import urljoin

from selenium.webdriver import Chrome
import pytest

from src.utils.pages import LoginPage, LandingPage
from src.utils import AdminApiClient
from src.utils.data_types import User


@pytest.fixture(scope="class")
def admin_client(base_url, admin_credentials):
    return AdminApiClient(base_url, **admin_credentials)


@pytest.fixture(scope="class")
def user(admin_client):
    _user = User(name="Susan", username=f"testuser-{uuid4()}", password="P4$$word")
    admin_client.create_user(_user)
    yield _user
    admin_client.delete_user(_user)


@pytest.fixture(scope="class")
def driver():
    _driver = Chrome()
    yield _driver
    _driver.quit()


@pytest.fixture(scope="class")
def landing_page(driver, login):
    return LandingPage(driver)


class TestLandingPageSuccess:
    @pytest.fixture(scope="class", autouse=True)
    @classmethod
    def login(cls, driver, base_url, user):
        driver.get(urljoin(base_url, "/login"))
        page = LoginPage(driver)
        page.login(user)

    def test_name_in_header(self, landing_page, user):
        assert landing_page.header == f"Welcome, {user.name}!"

    def test_sign_out_button(self, landing_page):
        assert landing_page.sign_out_button.is_displayed()

    def test_profile_link(self, landing_page, user):
        profile_href = urljoin(base_url, f"/profile?id={user.profile_id}")
        assert landing_page.profile_link.get_attribute("href") == profile_href
```

### Compact fixture with yield and teardown

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/fixtures.rst

A compact fixture that performs multiple setup steps and teardown in one function. It is harder to read, has a non-descriptive name, and cannot be reused easily. If any setup step raises an exception, the teardown code will not run.

```python
@pytest.fixture
def setup():
    mail_admin = MailAdminClient()
    sending_user = mail_admin.create_user()
    receiving_user = mail_admin.create_user()
    email = Email(subject="Hey!", body="How's it going?")
    sending_user.send_email(email, receiving_user)
    yield receiving_user, email
    receiving_user.clear_mailbox()
    mail_admin.delete_user(sending_user)
    mail_admin.delete_user(receiving_user)


def test_email_received(setup):
    receiving_user, email = setup
    assert email in receiving_user.inbox
```

--------------------------------

### Use module globals in skipif expression

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/announce/release-2.0.2.rst

Example of using a module global in a skipif expression. The test is skipped if the module's version string does not start with '1'. Using a string expression allows py.test to report meaningful skip/xfail conditions.

```python
import pytest
import mymodule
@pytest.mark.skipif("mymodule.__version__[0] == \"1\"")
def test_function():
    pass
```

--------------------------------

### Test class instance isolation example

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/getting-started.rst

Shows that each test in a class gets a unique instance, so class attributes are shared but instance attributes set in one test are not visible in another. The test_two fails because self.value is 0, not 1.

```python
# content of test_class_demo.py
class TestClassDemoInstance:
    value = 0

    def test_one(self):
        self.value = 1
        assert self.value == 1

    def test_two(self):
        assert self.value == 1
```

--------------------------------

### Manual equivalent of fixture requesting fixture

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/fixtures.rst

Manual equivalent of the fixture-requesting-fixture example, showing how pytest resolves the dependency chain by hand.

```python
def first_entry():
    return "a"


def order(first_entry):
    return [first_entry]


def test_string(order):
    # Act
    order.append("b")

    # Assert
    assert order == ["a", "b"]


entry = first_entry()
the_list = order(first_entry=entry)
test_string(order=the_list)
```

### test_step.py: incremental test class example

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/example/simple.rst

Example test module using the incremental marker on a class. When test_modification fails, test_deletion is not executed and is reported as an expected failure (xfail).

```python
# content of test_step.py

import pytest


@pytest.mark.incremental
class TestUserHandling:
    def test_login(self):
        pass

    def test_modification(self):
        assert 0

    def test_deletion(self):
        pass


def test_normal():
    pass
```

### Assertion failure output example

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/changelog.rst

Example of a failing test assertion output, showing the diff at index 0 and a hint to use -v for the full diff.

```python
def test():
    >       assert b'spam' == b'eggs'
    E       AssertionError: assert b'spam' == b'eggs'
    E         At index 0 diff: b's' != b'e'
    E         Use -v to get the full diff
```

--------------------------------

### Sphinx docstring format example

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/CONTRIBUTING.rst

Example of the Sphinx docstring format used in pytest's API reference. Shows how to document parameters, return values, exceptions, and version additions. Type hints in the signature are used instead of repeating types in annotations.

```python
def my_function(arg: ArgType) -> Foo:
    """Do important stuff.

    More detailed info here, in separate paragraphs from the subject line.
    Use proper sentences -- start sentences with capital letters and end
    with periods.

    Can include annotated documentation:

    :param short_arg: An argument which determines stuff.
    :param long_arg:
        A long explanation which spans multiple lines, overflows
        like this.
    :returns: The result.
    :raises ValueError:
        Detailed information when this can happen.

    .. versionadded:: 6.0

    Including types into the annotations above is not necessary when
    type-hinting is being used (as in this example).
    """
```

--------------------------------

### Safe fixture structure with separate fixtures

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/fixtures.rst

Safe fixture structure example: each fixture performs one state-changing action and bundles its teardown. It uses separate fixtures for admin client, user creation, browser driver, login, and landing page. The example assumes base_url and admin_credentials fixtures exist elsewhere.

```python
from uuid import uuid4
from urllib.parse import urljoin

from selenium.webdriver import Chrome
import pytest

from src.utils.pages import LoginPage, LandingPage
from src.utils import AdminApiClient
from src.utils.data_types import User


@pytest.fixture
def admin_client(base_url, admin_credentials):
    return AdminApiClient(base_url, **admin_credentials)


@pytest.fixture
def user(admin_client):
    _user = User(name="Susan", username=f"testuser-{uuid4()}", password="P4$$word")
    admin_client.create_user(_user)
    yield _user
    admin_client.delete_user(_user)


@pytest.fixture
def driver():
    _driver = Chrome()
    yield _driver
    _driver.quit()


@pytest.fixture
def login(driver, base_url, user):
    driver.get(urljoin(base_url, "/login"))
    page = LoginPage(driver)
    page.login(user)


@pytest.fixture
def landing_page(driver, login):
    return LandingPage(driver)


def test_name_on_landing_page_after_login(landing_page, user):
    assert landing_page.header == f"Welcome, {user.name}!"
```

### Example test using tmp_path fixture

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/tmp_path.rst

Shows how to use the tmp_path fixture to create a temporary directory and file within a test. The test creates a subdirectory, writes a file, and asserts its contents. The final assert 0 is intentionally included to demonstrate failure output.

```python
# content of test_tmp_path.py
CONTENT = "content"


def test_create_file(tmp_path):
    d = tmp_path / "sub"
    d.mkdir()
    p = d / "hello.txt"
    p.write_text(CONTENT, encoding="utf-8")
    assert p.read_text(encoding="utf-8") == CONTENT
    assert len(list(tmp_path.iterdir())) == 1
    assert 0
```

### Test module with callme methods and unittest example

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/example/special.rst

Shows test classes that define a callme classmethod, which will be called before any tests run. Also includes a unittest-based test class with a callme method to demonstrate compatibility.

```python
# content of test_module.py


class TestHello:
    @classmethod
    def callme(cls):
        print("callme called!")

    def test_method1(self):
        print("test_method1 called")

    def test_method2(self):
        print("test_method2 called")


class TestOther:
    @classmethod
    def callme(cls):
        print("callme other called")

    def test_other(self):
        print("test other")


# works with unittest as well ...
import unittest


class SomeTest(unittest.TestCase):
    @classmethod
    def callme(self):
        print("SomeTest callme called")

    def test_unit1(self):
        print("test_unit1 method called")
```

### test_simple.yaml example

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/example/nonpython.rst

A simple YAML file that defines two test cases: 'hello' and 'ok'. The 'hello' case has a spec where 'some' and 'other' are not equal, causing a failure. The 'ok' case has a spec where 'sub1' equals 'sub1', which passes.

```yaml
# test_simple.yaml
hello:
    some: other
ok:
    sub1: sub1
```

### Test plugin using pytester fixture

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/writing_plugins.rst

Use the pytester fixture to create a temporary conftest.py file and run tests. This example creates a conftest with a parametrized fixture named 'name'.

```python
def test_hello(pytester):
    """Make sure that our plugin works."""

    # create a temporary conftest.py file
    pytester.makeconftest(
        """
        import pytest

        @pytest.fixture(params=[
            "Brianna",
            "Andreas",
            "Floris",
        ])
        def name(request):
            return request.param
    """
    )
```

--------------------------------

### Problematic code: non-Collection iterable in @pytest.mark.parametrize

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/deprecations.rst

Deprecated in 9.1. Using non-Collection iterables (like generators) for argvalues in parametrize is deprecated because they get exhausted after the first iteration, causing tests to be skipped. This example shows a generator passed directly, leading to test_2 being skipped.

```python
import pytest


def data_generator():
    yield 1
    yield 2


@pytest.mark.parametrize("n", data_generator())
class Test:
    def test_1(self, n):
        pass

    # test_2 will be skipped because data_generator() is exhausted.
    def test_2(self, n):
        pass
```

--------------------------------

### Class level setup and teardown with setup_class and teardown_class

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/xunit_setup.rst

Define class methods setup_class and teardown_class to set up and tear down state before and after all test methods of a class are called. These are called at class level.

```python
@classmethod
def setup_class(cls):
    """setup any state specific to the execution of the given class (which
    usually contains tests).
    """


@classmethod
def teardown_class(cls):
    """teardown any state that was previously setup with a call to
    setup_class.
    """
```

--------------------------------

### pytest -m mark expression examples

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/reference/reference.rst

Examples of using the -m option to run tests matching mark expressions. Supports and, or, and not operators; marker names are matched exactly and case-sensitively.

```bash
pytest -m slow                   # run tests marked with @pytest.mark.slow
pytest -m "not slow"             # run tests NOT marked slow
pytest -m "mark1 and not mark2"  # run tests marked mark1 but not mark2
pytest -m "device(serial='123')" # run tests marked device with that argument
```

```python
retcode = pytest.main(["-x", "mytestdir"])
```

### Match warning messages with pytest.warns

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/capture-warnings.rst

Examples of using the match keyword with pytest.warns to assert warning messages via text or regex. Includes a failing case and an example using re.escape for literal strings with metacharacters.

```pycon
>>> with warns(UserWarning, match="must be 0 or None"):
...     warnings.warn("value must be 0 or None", UserWarning)
...

>>> with warns(UserWarning, match=r"must be \d+$"):
...     warnings.warn("value must be 42", UserWarning)
...

>>> with warns(UserWarning, match=r"must be \d+$"):
...     warnings.warn("this is not here", UserWarning)
...
Traceback (most recent call last):
  ...
Failed: Regex pattern did not match any of the 1 warnings emitted.
 Regex: ...
 Emitted warnings: ...UserWarning...

>>> with warns(UserWarning, match=re.escape("issue with foo() func")):
...     warnings.warn("issue with foo() func")
...
```

### Skip/xfail with parametrize

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/skipping.rst

Example of applying skip and xfail markers to individual test instances using pytest.param within parametrize.

```python
import sys

import pytest


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    ("n", "expected"),
    [
        (1, 2),
        pytest.param(1, 0, marks=pytest.mark.xfail),
        pytest.param(1, 3, marks=pytest.mark.xfail(reason="some bug")),
        (2, 3),
        (3, 4),
        (4, 5),
        pytest.param(
            10, 11, marks=pytest.mark.skipif(sys.version_info >= (3, 0), reason="py2k")
        ),
    ],
)
def test_increment(n, expected):
    assert n + 1 == expected
```

### pytest -k keyword expression examples

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/reference/reference.rst

Examples of using the -k option to filter tests by keyword expressions. Expressions combine names with and, or, not and parentheses, matched case-insensitively as substrings.

```bash
pytest -k "test_method or test_other"  # matches keywords containing 'test_method' OR 'test_other'
pytest -k "not test_method"            # matches keywords NOT containing 'test_method'
pytest -k "not test_method and not test_other"  # excludes both
```

--------------------------------

### Fixture scoping example: tests/test_top.py and tests/subpackage/

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/reference/fixtures.rst

This example shows how fixtures are scoped to directories. The top-level test file defines an 'innermost' fixture, while the subpackage defines its own 'innermost' fixture that depends on a 'mid' fixture from the subpackage's conftest.py. The test in the subpackage uses the subpackage's 'innermost' fixture, not the top-level one, because it cannot search downward.

```python
@pytest.fixture
            def order():
                return []

            @pytest.fixture
            def top(order, innermost):
                order.append("top")
```

```python
# content of tests/test_top.py
            import pytest

            @pytest.fixture
            def innermost(order):
                order.append("innermost top")

            def test_order(order, top):
                assert order == ["innermost top", "top"]
```

```python
# content of tests/subpackage/conftest.py
                import pytest

                @pytest.fixture
                def mid(order):
                    order.append("mid subpackage")
```

```python
# content of tests/subpackage/test_subpackage.py
                import pytest

                @pytest.fixture
                def innermost(order, mid):
                    order.append("innermost subpackage")

                def test_order(order, top):
                    assert order == ["mid subpackage", "innermost subpackage", "top"]
```

--------------------------------

### Replace cached_setup with fixture yield

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/deprecations.rst

Replacement for request.cached_setup using standard fixture mechanisms with yield.

```python
@pytest.fixture(scope="module")
def db_session():
    session = Session.create()
    yield session
    session.close()
```

--------------------------------

### Make tests a package with __init__.py to allow duplicate test module names

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/explanation/goodpractices.rst

Adds __init__.py files to the tests directory and subdirectories to turn them into packages, allowing test modules with the same name to be imported as tests.foo.test_view and tests.bar.test_view. Note that this prepends the repository root to sys.path, making the local mypkg importable, which can be problematic when testing an installed version with tox.

```text
pyproject.toml
mypkg/
    ...
tests/
    __init__.py
    foo/
        __init__.py
        test_view.py
    bar/
        __init__.py
        test_view.py
```

--------------------------------

### Store data in item.stash during pytest_runtest_setup

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/writing_hook_functions.rst

Uses the stash keys to store data on the item during the setup phase.

```python
def pytest_runtest_setup(item: pytest.Item) -> None:
    item.stash[been_there_key] = True
    item.stash[done_that_key] = "no"
```

--------------------------------

### Port nose @with_setup to pytest fixture

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/deprecations.rst

Pytest fixture-based replacement for the nose @with_setup decorator. The fixture calls setup_some_resource before the test and teardown_some_resource after it, using yield to separate setup and teardown phases.

```python
import pytest


def setup_some_resource(): ...


def teardown_some_resource(): ...


@pytest.fixture
def some_resource():
    setup_some_resource()
    yield
    teardown_some_resource()


def test_foo(some_resource): ...
```

### Replace nose setup/teardown with pytest setup_method/teardown_method

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/deprecations.rst

Replaces nose-style setup/teardown methods with pytest's xunit-style setup_method and teardown_method. The original nose code used setup() and teardown() methods; this snippet shows the equivalent pytest structure.

```python
class Test:
    def setup_method(self):
        self.resource = make_resource()

    def teardown_method(self):
        self.resource.close()

    def test_foo(self): ...

    def test_bar(self): ...
```

### Manual equivalent of fixture request

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/fixtures.rst

Manual equivalent of the previous example, showing how pytest's fixture invocation works under the hood: the fixture is called and its result is passed to the test function.

```python
def fruit_bowl():
    return [Fruit("apple"), Fruit("banana")]


def test_fruit_salad(fruit_bowl):
    # Act
    fruit_salad = FruitSalad(*fruit_bowl)

    # Assert
    assert all(fruit.cubed for fruit in fruit_salad.fruit)


# Arrange
bowl = fruit_bowl()
test_fruit_salad(fruit_bowl=bowl)
```

### Method level setup and teardown with setup_method and teardown_method

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/xunit_setup.rst

Define setup_method and teardown_method in a test class to set up and tear down state around each test method invocation. As of pytest-3.0, the method parameter is optional.

```python
def setup_method(self, method):
    """setup any state tied to the execution of the given method in a
    class.  setup_method is invoked for every test method of a class.
    """


def teardown_method(self, method):
    """teardown any state that was previously setup with a setup_method
    call.
    """
```

### Write a test function using the acceptance fixture

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/example/attic.rst

Shows a test that uses the accept fixture to create a subdirectory and run a command, asserting the output contains the subdirectory name. Without the -A option, the test is skipped.

```python
def test_some_acceptance_aspect(accept):
    accept.tmpdir.mkdir("somesub")
    result = accept.run("ls", "-la")
    assert "somesub" in result
```

### Requesting a fixture in multiple tests

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/fixtures.rst

Shows a fixture being requested by multiple tests and how the fixture's return value is passed to each test. The example includes two test functions that both use the 'order' fixture, and demonstrates calling the fixture function directly to simulate the test execution.

```python
def first_entry():
    return "a"


def order(first_entry):
    return [first_entry]


def test_string(order):
    # Act
    order.append("b")

    # Assert
    assert order == ["a", "b"]


def test_int(order):
    # Act
    order.append(2)

    # Assert
    assert order == ["a", 2]


entry = first_entry()
the_list = order(first_entry=entry)
test_string(order=the_list)

entry = first_entry()
the_list = order(first_entry=entry)
test_int(order=the_list)
```

### Create first test in test_sample.py

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/getting-started.rst

Create a simple test file with a function and a test that asserts the function's output. The test is expected to fail because func(3) returns 4, not 5.

```python
# content of test_sample.py
def func(x):
    return x + 1


def test_answer():
    assert func(3) == 5
```

### Fixture search with plugins: tests/conftest.py and tests/subpackage/

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/reference/fixtures.rst

This example shows how fixtures from third-party plugins are searched last. The conftest.py defines an 'order' fixture, the subpackage conftest.py defines an autouse 'mid' fixture that depends on 'b_fix' from plugin_b, and the test file defines an 'inner' fixture that depends on 'mid' and 'a_fix' from plugin_a. The test asserts the order of fixture execution.

```python
# content of tests/conftest.py
            import pytest

            @pytest.fixture
            def order():
                return []
```

```python
# content of tests/subpackage/conftest.py
                import pytest

                @pytest.fixture(autouse=True)
                def mid(order, b_fix):
                    order.append("mid subpackage")
```

```python
# content of tests/subpackage/test_subpackage.py
                import pytest

                @pytest.fixture
                def inner(order, mid, a_fix):
                    order.append("inner subpackage")

                def test_order(order, inner):
                    assert order == ["b_fix", "mid subpackage", "a_fix", "inner subpackage"]
```

### py.cleanup command examples

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/changelog.rst

Command-line usage of py.cleanup to remove various file types and directories. Options include -e for extra extensions, -s for build/dist directories, -d for empty directories, -a as a synonym for combined options, and -n for a dry run.

```shell
py.cleanup     # remove "*.pyc" and "*$py.class" (jython) files
py.cleanup -e .swp -e .cache # also remove files with these extensions
py.cleanup -s  # remove "build" and "dist" directory next to setup.py files
py.cleanup -d  # also remove empty directories
py.cleanup -a  # synonym for "-s -d -e 'pip-log.txt'"
py.cleanup -n  # dry run, only show what would be removed
```

### Correct assert in test function

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/assert.rst

Correct fix for the previous example: replace the return statement with an assert so the test fails when the condition is false.

```python
@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    ["a", "b", "result"],
    [
        [1, 2, 5],
        [2, 3, 8],
        [5, 3, 18],
    ],
)
def test_foo(a, b, result):
    assert foo(a, b) == result
```

### Old-style funcarg with cached_setup for session-scoped Database

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/funcarg_compare.rst

Shows the pre-pytest-2.3 way to create a per-session Database object using pytest_funcarg__ and request.cached_setup(). Requires understanding of cached_setup() mechanics and does not support direct parametrization.

```python
# content of conftest.py
class Database:
    def __init__(self):
        print("database instance created")

    def destroy(self):
        print("database instance destroyed")


def pytest_funcarg__db(request):
    return request.cached_setup(
        setup=DataBase, teardown=lambda db: db.destroy, scope="session"
    )
```

### Apply skipif to a class

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/skipping.rst

Use the skipif marker on a class to skip all its test methods when the condition is True. The example skips the class on Windows.

```python
@pytest.mark.skipif(sys.platform == "win32", reason="does not run on windows")
class TestPosixCalls:
    def test_function(self):
        "will not be setup or run under 'win32' platform"
```

### Original connection string code - app.py

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/monkeypatch.rst

Defines a DEFAULT_CONFIG dictionary and a function create_connection_string that builds a connection string from a config, defaulting to DEFAULT_CONFIG. This is the code under test for the dictionary monkeypatching examples.

```python
# contents of app.py to generate a simple connection string
DEFAULT_CONFIG = {"user": "user1", "database": "db1"}


def create_connection_string(config=None):
    """Creates a connection string from input or defaults."""
    config = config or DEFAULT_CONFIG
    return f"User Id={config['user']}; Location={config['database']};"
```

```python
def test_increment(input_value: int, expected_output: int) -> None:
    assert input_value + 1 == expected_output
```

### Module level setup and teardown with setup_module and teardown_module

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/xunit_setup.rst

Define setup_module and teardown_module in a test module to set up and tear down state once for all test functions and classes in that module. As of pytest-3.0, the module parameter is optional.

```python
def setup_module(module):
    """setup any state specific to the execution of the given module."""


def teardown_module(module):
    """teardown any state that was previously setup with a setup_module
    method.
    """
```

### Configuration file search order

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/reference/customize.rst

Shows the order in which pytest checks for configuration files, starting from the common ancestor directory and moving upwards. Comments indicate the required content for each file to match.

```text
# first look for path/pytest.toml
path/pytest.toml
path/pytest.ini
path/pyproject.toml  # must contain a [tool.pytest] table to match
path/tox.ini         # must contain [pytest] section to match
path/setup.cfg       # must contain [tool:pytest] section to match
pytest.toml
pytest.ini
... # all the way up to the root

# now look for setup.py
path/setup.py
setup.py
... # all the way up to the root
```

### Specify multiple fixtures with usefixtures marker, pytestmark, and pytest.toml

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/fixtures.rst

Shows three ways to specify multiple fixtures: passing multiple fixture names to @pytest.mark.usefixtures, setting the module-level pytestmark variable, and configuring usefixtures in a pytest.toml file. The pytest.toml example applies the fixture to all tests in the project.

```python
@pytest.mark.usefixtures("cleandir", "anotherfixture")
```

```python
pytestmark = pytest.mark.usefixtures("cleandir")
```

```toml
# content of pytest.toml
```

### Command line construction with addopts and PYTEST_ADDOPTS

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/example/simple.rst

This text shows the order in which pytest builds the command line: configuration file addopts, then PYTEST_ADDOPTS, then extra command line arguments. Conflicting options are resolved with the last one winning.

```text
<configuration file addopts> $PYTEST_ADDOPTS <extra command-line arguments>
```

### Access exception information via sys.last_*

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/failures.rst

After a failure, the exception information is stored in sys.last_value, sys.last_type, and sys.last_traceback. This example shows how to manually access them in interactive use.

```python
>>> import sys
>>> sys.last_traceback.tb_lineno
42
>>> sys.last_value
AssertionError('assert result == "ok"',)
```

### INI configuration for filterwarnings

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/capture-warnings.rst

INI configuration example for the filterwarnings option. It ignores all user warnings and specific deprecation warnings matching a regex, but transforms all other warnings into errors.

```ini
[pytest]
filterwarnings =
    error
    ignore::UserWarning
    ignore:function ham\(\) is deprecated:DeprecationWarning
```

### Create parametrized test with 50 invocations, 2 failing

```python
# content of test_50.py
import pytest


@pytest.mark.parametrize("i", range(50))
def test_num(i):
    if i in (17, 25):
        pytest.fail("bad luck")
```

### Register custom markers in pytest_configure

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/writing_plugins.rst

Register markers in pytest_configure to make them appear in help text and avoid spurious warnings. The example registers 'cool_marker' and 'mark_with'.

```python
def pytest_configure(config):
    config.addinivalue_line("markers", "cool_marker: this one is for cool tests.")
    config.addinivalue_line(
        "markers", "mark_with(arg, arg2): this marker takes arguments."
    )
```

### pytest_generate_tests example with test and conftest

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/parametrize.rst

Implements a custom parametrization scheme using the pytest_generate_tests hook. The test function accepts a stringinput fixture, and conftest.py adds a command-line option and parametrizes the test based on it. Running with two stringinput values runs the test twice.

```python
# content of test_strings.py


def test_valid_string(stringinput):
    assert stringinput.isalpha()
```

```python
# content of conftest.py


def pytest_addoption(parser):
    parser.addoption(
        "--stringinput",
        action="append",
        default=[],
        help="list of stringinputs to pass to test functions",
    )


def pytest_generate_tests(metafunc):
    if "stringinput" in metafunc.fixturenames:
        metafunc.parametrize("stringinput", metafunc.config.getoption("stringinput"))
```

### python_files in pyproject.toml (TOML)

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/reference/reference.rst

Sets glob-style file patterns for test modules. The example includes multiple patterns in a list.

```toml
[pytest]
python_files = ["test_*.py", "check_*.py", "example_*.py"]
```

### Drop to PDB on first failure or first three failures

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/failures.rst

Use -x --pdb to drop to PDB on the first failure and then end the test session, or --pdb --maxfail=3 to drop to PDB for the first three failures.

```bash
pytest -x --pdb   # drop to PDB on first failure, then end test session
pytest --pdb --maxfail=3  # drop to PDB for first three failures
```

--------------------------------

### Define and use fixtures with @pytest.fixture

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/explanation/fixtures.rst

Shows a basic fixture setup: a Fruit class, a my_fruit fixture returning an apple, a fruit_basket fixture that depends on my_fruit, and a test that checks the fruit is in the basket. Fixtures are accessed via test function arguments.

```python
import pytest


class Fruit:
    def __init__(self, name):
        self.name = name

    def __eq__(self, other):
        return self.name == other.name


@pytest.fixture
def my_fruit():
    return Fruit("apple")


@pytest.fixture
def fruit_basket(my_fruit):
    return [Fruit("banana"), my_fruit]


def test_my_fruit_in_basket(my_fruit, fruit_basket):
    assert my_fruit in fruit_basket
```

--------------------------------

### Share skipif markers between modules

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/skipping.rst

Define a skipif marker in one module and import it in another to reuse the same skip condition. The example defines a marker in test_mymodule.py and uses it in test_myothermodule.py.

```python
# content of test_mymodule.py
import mymodule

minversion = pytest.mark.skipif(
    mymodule.__versioninfo__ < (1, 1), reason="at least mymodule-1.1 required"
)


@minversion
def test_function(): ...
```

```python
# test_myothermodule.py
from test_mymodule import minversion


@minversion
def test_anotherfunction(): ...
```

--------------------------------

### Function level setup and teardown with setup_function and teardown_function

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/xunit_setup.rst

Define setup_function and teardown_function at module level to set up and tear down state for each test function defined directly at module level. As of pytest-3.0, the function parameter is optional.

```python
def setup_function(function):
    """setup any state tied to the execution of the given function.
    Invoked for every test function in the module.
    """


def teardown_function(function):
    """teardown any state that was previously setup with a setup_function
    call.
    """
```

### pyproject.toml build-system and project configuration

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/explanation/goodpractices.rst

Create a pyproject.toml file in the root of your repository. Replace PACKAGENAME and PACKAGEVERSION with your package's name and version.

```toml
[build-system]
requires = ["hatchling"]
build-backend = "hatchling.build"

[project]
name = "PACKAGENAME"
version = "PACKAGEVERSION"
```

--------------------------------

### Reusable fixture with two tests

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/fixtures.rst

Shows that fixtures are reusable: two tests request the same 'order' fixture, and each gets its own fresh list object, so tests are isolated.

```python
# contents of test_append.py
import pytest


# Arrange
@pytest.fixture
def first_entry():
    return "a"


# Arrange
@pytest.fixture
def order(first_entry):
    return [first_entry]


def test_string(order):
    # Act
    order.append("b")

    # Assert
    assert order == ["a", "b"]


def test_int(order):
    # Act
    order.append(2)

    # Assert
    assert order == ["a", 2]
```

### Skip an entire module with pytest.skip(allow_module_level=True)

```python
if not sys.platform.startswith("win"):
```

### Using matches method for manual matching

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/assert.rst

Shows how to use the matches method of RaisesGroup and RaisesExc for matching outside of a context manager, and how to access the fail_reason attribute for helpful error messages.

```python
def test_matches():
    exc = ValueError()
    exc_group = ExceptionGroup("", [exc])
    if RaisesGroup(ValueError).matches(exc_group):
        ...
    # helpful error is available in `.fail_reason` if it fails to match
    r = RaisesExc(ValueError)
    assert r.matches(e), r.fail_reason
```

```python
def pytest_funcarg__arg(request):

def test_function(arg):
    ...
```

### Invalid use of usefixtures on a fixture function

This is an error: @pytest.mark.usefixtures cannot be used on fixture functions. The example shows an attempt to apply the marker to a fixture, which will not work.

```python
@pytest.mark.usefixtures("my_other_fixture")
    @pytest.fixture
    def my_fixture_that_sadly_wont_use_my_other_fixture(): ...
```

--------------------------------

### Use usefixtures marker to apply cleandir fixture to a test class

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/fixtures.rst

Shows a fixture that changes the current working directory to a temporary directory and restores it after the test, and how to apply it to a test class using the @pytest.mark.usefixtures marker. The test class verifies that the directory starts empty and that a file can be created. Running pytest -q produces the output shown.

```python
# content of test_ids.py

    @pytest.fixture
    def cleandir():
        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as newpath:
            old_cwd = os.getcwd()
            os.chdir(newpath)
            yield
            os.chdir(old_cwd)
```

```python
# content of test_setenv.py
    import os

    import pytest


    @pytest.mark.usefixtures("cleandir")
    class TestDirectoryInit:
        def test_cwd_starts_empty(self):
            assert os.listdir(os.getcwd()) == []
            with open("myfile", "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
                f.write("hello")

        def test_cwd_again_starts_empty(self):
            assert os.listdir(os.getcwd()) == []
```

```pytest
$ pytest -q
    ..                                                                   [100%]
    2 passed in 0.12s
```

--------------------------------

### Original code using os.getenv - code.py

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/monkeypatch.rst

The original code file (code.py) contains a function get_os_user_lower() that reads the USER environment variable and raises OSError if not set. This is the code under test for the environment variable monkeypatching examples.

```python
# contents of our original code file e.g. code.py
import os


def get_os_user_lower():
    """Simple retrieval function.
    Returns lowercase USER or raises OSError."""
    username = os.getenv("USER")

    if username is None:
        raise OSError("USER environment is not set.")

    return username.lower()
```

### Clone and set up pytest repository

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/CONTRIBUTING.rst

Clone the pytest repository, fetch tags, and create a branch for your changes. The second block adds the upstream repository and fetches its tags to ensure you have all tags locally.

```bash
$ git clone git@github.com:YOUR_GITHUB_USERNAME/pytest.git
$ cd pytest
$ git fetch --tags https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest
# now, create your own branch off "main":

    $ git checkout -b your-bugfix-branch-name main
```

```bash
$ git remote add upstream https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest
$ git fetch upstream --tags
```

### test_verbosity_example.py sample test file

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/output.rst

A simple test file with four tests: one passing and three failing (list comparison, dict comparison, and substring check). Used to demonstrate verbosity output.

```python
# content of test_verbosity_example.py
def test_ok():
    pass


def test_words_fail():
    fruits1 = ["banana", "apple", "grapes", "melon", "kiwi"]
    fruits2 = ["banana", "apple", "orange", "melon", "kiwi"]
    assert fruits1 == fruits2


def test_numbers_fail():
    number_to_text1 = {str(x): x for x in range(5)}
    number_to_text2 = {str(x * 10): x * 10 for x in range(5)}
    assert number_to_text1 == number_to_text2


def test_long_text_fail():
    long_text = "Lorem ipsum dolor sit amet " * 10
    assert "hello world" in long_text
```

### Imperatively mark a test as XFAIL with pytest.xfail

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/skipping.rst

Call pytest.xfail from within a test or its setup function to mark the test as XFAIL. No other code is executed after the call, because it is implemented by raising an exception.

```python
def test_function():
    if not valid_config():
        pytest.xfail("failing configuration (but should work)")
```

### Defer hook implementation to a conditional plugin

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/writing_hook_functions.rst

Defines a plugin class that implements a standard xdist hook function and registers it only if the xdist plugin is present, avoiding validation failures when the plugin is not installed.

```python
# contents of myplugin.py


class DeferPlugin:
    """Simple plugin to defer pytest-xdist hook functions."""

    def pytest_testnodedown(self, node, error):
        """standard xdist hook function."""


def pytest_configure(config):
    if config.pluginmanager.hasplugin("xdist"):
        config.pluginmanager.register(DeferPlugin())
```

--------------------------------

### Hook wrapper example with @pytest.hookimpl(wrapper=True)

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/writing_hook_functions.rst

A hook wrapper is a generator function that yields exactly once. It can post-process the result, handle exceptions, or return (yield) for simple side effects.

```python
import pytest


@pytest.hookimpl(wrapper=True)
def pytest_pyfunc_call(pyfuncitem):
    do_something_before_next_hook_executes()

    # If the outcome is an exception, will raise the exception.
    res = yield

    new_res = post_process_result(res)

    # Override the return value to the plugin system.
    return new_res
```

### Example of improved pytest.approx assertion message

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/changelog.rst

Shows the improved assertion message for pytest.approx when comparing sequences of numbers. The message includes a table with the index and error for each diff.

```text
>       assert [1, 2, 3, 4] == pytest.approx([1, 3, 3, 5])
E       assert comparison failed for 2 values:
E         Index | Obtained | Expected
E         1     | 2        | 3 +- 3.0e-06
E         3     | 4        | 5 +- 5.0e-06
```

### Limit stdlib patching with monkeypatch.context

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/monkeypatch.rst

Use monkeypatch.context() to limit patching to a specific block. In this example, the stdlib functools.partial is replaced, but the change is automatically reverted after the with block ends, preventing side effects on other tests.

```python
import functools


def test_partial(monkeypatch):
    with monkeypatch.context() as m:
        m.setattr(functools, "partial", 3)
        assert functools.partial == 3
```

### Run pytest -ra to produce a detailed summary report

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/output.rst

Use the -r flag with the 'a' character to display a short test summary info at the end of the test session, listing all failures, errors, skips, xfails, and xpasses. The default is 'fE' to list failures and errors. The example shows a test file with various outcomes and the resulting output when running pytest -ra.

```python
# content of test_example.py
import pytest


@pytest.fixture
def error_fixture():
    assert 0


def test_ok():
    print("ok")


def test_fail():
    assert 0


def test_error(error_fixture):
    pass


def test_skip():
    pytest.skip("skipping this test")


def test_xfail():
    pytest.xfail("xfailing this test")


@pytest.mark.xfail(reason="always xfail")
def test_xpass():
    pass

```

### Using subtests to check all Python files for docstrings

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/changelog.rst

Example from the pytest 9.0.0 changelog showing how to use the new subtests feature. It iterates over all .py files in the current directory and uses the subtests context manager to report each assertion failure individually.

```python
def contains_docstring(p: Path) -> bool:
    """Return True if the given Python file contains a top-level docstring."""
    ...


def test_py_files_contain_docstring(subtests: pytest.Subtests) -> None:
    for path in Path.cwd().glob("*.py"):
        with subtests.test(path=str(path)):
            assert contains_docstring(path)
```

```python
import sys


@pytest.mark.skipif(sys.version_info < (3, 13), reason="requires python3.13 or higher")
def test_function(): ...
```

### Declare a new hook with pytest_my_hook

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/writing_hook_functions.rst

Declares a new hook named pytest_my_hook. The function name must start with 'pytest_' and the body is typically a do-nothing placeholder with documentation.

```python
def pytest_my_hook(config):
    """
    Receives the pytest config and does things with it
    """
```

### pytest.approx output example (previous and current)

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/changelog.rst

Shows the previous and current console output of pytest.approx for a value with an absolute tolerance. The previous output used scientific notation (42 ± 1.0e+00), while the current output uses decimal notation (42 ± 1).

```console
>>> pytest.approx(42, abs=1)
42 ± 1.0e+00
```

```console
>>> pytest.approx(42, abs=1)
42 ± 1
```

### Port testscenarios to pytest with pytest_generate_tests

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/example/parametrize.rst

Fully self-contained example that adapts testscenarios-style scenarios to pytest by constructing arguments for Metafunc.parametrize. Uses `scope="class"` to make scenario values class-scoped, which affects resource-based ordering.

```python
# content of test_scenarios.py


def pytest_generate_tests(metafunc):
    idlist = []
    argvalues = []
    for scenario in metafunc.cls.scenarios:
        idlist.append(scenario[0])
        items = scenario[1].items()
        argnames = [x[0] for x in items]
        argvalues.append([x[1] for x in items])
    metafunc.parametrize(argnames, argvalues, ids=idlist, scope="class")


scenario1 = ("basic", {"attribute": "value"})
scenario2 = ("advanced", {"attribute": "value2"})


class TestSampleWithScenarios:
    scenarios = [scenario1, scenario2]

    def test_demo1(self, attribute):
        assert isinstance(attribute, str)

    def test_demo2(self, attribute):
        assert isinstance(attribute, str)
```

--------------------------------

### pytest verbosity options

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/output.rst

Command-line options to control verbosity: --quiet/-q for less verbose, -v to increase verbosity, -vv for more detail, and -vvv for even more detail in certain setups.

```bash
pytest --quiet          # quiet - less verbose - mode
pytest -q               # quiet - less verbose - mode (shortcut)
pytest -v               # increase verbosity, display individual test names
pytest -vv              # more verbose, display more details from the test output
pytest -vvv             # not a standard , but may be used for even more detail in certain setups
```

Example of a test method that formally defines self as positional-only and fixture parameters as keyword-only. Before this fix, such methods caused an internal error in pytest.

```python
class TestClass:

    def test_method(self, /, *, fixture): ...
```

### TOML configuration for filterwarnings

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/capture-warnings.rst

TOML configuration example for the filterwarnings option. It ignores all user warnings and specific deprecation warnings matching a regex, but transforms all other warnings into errors. Note the use of single quotes to denote raw strings in TOML.

```toml
[pytest]
filterwarnings = [
    'error',
    'ignore::UserWarning',
    # Note the use of single quote below to denote "raw" strings in TOML.
    'ignore:function ham\(\) is deprecated:DeprecationWarning',
]
```

--------------------------------

### Fixture-based environment monkeypatching - test_code.py

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/monkeypatch.rst

Moves the environment variable monkeypatching into pytest fixtures (mock_env_user and mock_env_missing) and references them in tests. This shares the setup across multiple tests without duplicating the monkeypatch calls.

```python
# contents of our test file e.g. test_code.py
import pytest


@pytest.fixture
    def mock_env_user(monkeypatch):
        monkeypatch.setenv("USER", "TestingUser")


@pytest.fixture
    def mock_env_missing(monkeypatch):
        monkeypatch.delenv("USER", raising=False)


    # notice the tests reference the fixtures for mocks
    def test_upper_to_lower(mock_env_user):
        assert get_os_user_lower() == "testinguser"


    def test_raise_exception(mock_env_missing):
        with pytest.raises(OSError):
            _ = get_os_user_lower()
```

--------------------------------

### Dynamically modify command line arguments in pytest_load_initial_conftests

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/example/simple.rst

An external plugin that dynamically adds -n option to args if pytest-xdist is installed. It sets the number of subprocesses to half the CPU count, modifying args in place.

```python
# installable external plugin
import sys


def pytest_load_initial_conftests(args):
    if "xdist" in sys.modules:  # pytest-xdist plugin
        import multiprocessing

        num = max(multiprocessing.cpu_count() / 2, 1)
        args[:] = ["-n", str(num)] + args
```

### Multiple subtests and normal assertions in one test

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/subtests.rst

Shows using subtests multiple times in the same test and mixing with normal assertions outside the subtests.test block. The example uses two stages with different ranges and a normal assertion between them.

```python
def test(subtests):
    for i in range(5):
        with subtests.test("stage 1", i=i):
            assert i % 2 == 0

    assert func() == 10

    for i in range(10, 20):
        with subtests.test("stage 2", i=i):
            assert i % 2 == 0
```

### Imperatively mark a test as XFAIL with pytest.xfail (slow module)

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/skipping.rst

Another example of using pytest.xfail imperatively, this time based on a slow module's function result. The test will be marked XFAIL and no further code executes after the call.

```python
def test_function2():
    import slow_module

    if slow_module.slow_function():
        pytest.xfail("slow_module taking too long")
```

### conftest.py with indirect parametrization and db fixture

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/example/parametrize.rst

Adds a test configuration that generates two invocations of `test_db_initialized` and implements a factory creating database objects for actual test invocations. Uses `indirect=True` to defer resource setup until test run.

```python
# content of conftest.py
import pytest


def pytest_generate_tests(metafunc):
    if "db" in metafunc.fixturenames:
        metafunc.parametrize("db", ["d1", "d2"], indirect=True)


class DB1:
    "one database object"


class DB2:
    "alternative database object"


@pytest.fixture
def db(request):
    if request.param == "d1":
        return DB1()
    elif request.param == "d2":
        return DB2()
    else:
        raise ValueError("invalid internal test config")
```

Use pytest.importorskip at module level, within a test, or in test setup to skip the test if the import fails. If the module cannot be imported, the test will be skipped.

```python
docutils = pytest.importorskip("docutils")
```

### Factory as fixture with cleanup

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/fixtures.rst

The factory-as-fixture pattern with cleanup: the fixture yields the factory and then destroys all created records after the test. Uses models.Customer and assumes a destroy method.

```python
@pytest.fixture
def make_customer_record():
    created_records = []

    def _make_customer_record(name):
        record = models.Customer(name=name, orders=[])
        created_records.append(record)
        return record

    yield _make_customer_record

    for record in created_records:
        record.destroy()


def test_customer_records(make_customer_record):
    customer_1 = make_customer_record("Lisa")
    customer_2 = make_customer_record("Mike")
    customer_3 = make_customer_record("Meredith")

```

```toml

```

### Define acceptance test option and fixture in conftest.py

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/example/attic.rst

Adds a -A command line option and a funcarg fixture that skips tests unless the option is given. The fixture creates a temporary directory and provides a run() method to execute commands.

```python
# ./conftest.py
    def pytest_option(parser):
        group = parser.getgroup("myproject")
        group.addoption(
            "-A", dest="acceptance", action="store_true", help="run (slow) acceptance tests"
        )


    def pytest_funcarg__accept(request):
        return AcceptFixture(request)


    class AcceptFixture:
        def __init__(self, request):
            if not request.config.getoption("acceptance"):
                pytest.skip("specify -A to run acceptance tests")
            self.tmpdir = request.config.mktemp(request.function.__name__, numbered=True)

        def run(self, *cmd):
            """called by test code to execute an acceptance test."""
            self.tmpdir.chdir()
            return subprocess.check_output(cmd).decode()
```

### Using config.cache in a fixture

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/cache.rst

Example plugin using the config.cache object to reuse previously created state across pytest invocations. It checks if the cache attribute exists (if not, the cache plugin is disabled), retrieves a value with cache.get, and stores it with cache.set.

```python
# content of test_caching.py
import pytest


def expensive_computation():
    print("running expensive computation...")


@pytest.fixture
    def mydata(pytestconfig):
        cache = getattr(pytestconfig, "cache", None)
        if cache is None:
            # pytestconfig not having the cache attribute means the
            # cache plugin is disabled.
            expensive_computation()
            return 42

        val = cache.get("example/value", None)
        if val is None:
            expensive_computation()
            val = 42
            cache.set("example/value", val)
        return val


def test_function(mydata):
    assert mydata == 23
```

### Make test result information available in fixtures with StashKey

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/example/simple.rst

A local plugin (conftest.py) that stores test result reports per phase using StashKey and a fixture that prints failure/skip status in its finalizer. The test module and the pytest -s output show how setup failures and call failures are reported.

```python
# content of conftest.py
import pytest
from pytest import StashKey, CollectReport

phase_report_key = StashKey[dict[str, CollectReport]]()


@pytest.hookimpl(wrapper=True, tryfirst=True)
def pytest_runtest_makereport(item, call):
    # execute all other hooks to obtain the report object
    rep = yield

    # store test results for each phase of a call, which can
    # be "setup", "call", "teardown"
    item.stash.setdefault(phase_report_key, {})[rep.when] = rep

    return rep


@pytest.fixture
def something(request):
    yield
    # request.node is an "item" because we use the default
    # "function" scope
    report = request.node.stash[phase_report_key]
    if report["setup"].failed:
        print("setting up a test failed", request.node.nodeid)
    elif report["setup"].skipped:
        print("setting up a test skipped", request.node.nodeid)
    elif ("call" not in report) or report["call"].failed:
        print("executing test failed or skipped", request.node.nodeid)
```

```python
# content of test_module.py

import pytest


@pytest.fixture
def other():
    assert 0


def test_setup_fails(something, other):
    pass


def test_call_fails(something):
    assert 0


def test_fail2():
    assert 0
```

```pytest
$ pytest -s test_module.py
=========================== test session starts ============================
platform linux -- Python 3.x.y, pytest-9.x.y, pluggy-1.x.y
rootdir: /home/sweet/project
collected 3 items

test_module.py Esetting up a test failed test_module.py::test_setup_fails
Fexecuting test failed or skipped test_module.py::test_call_fails
F

================================== ERRORS ==================================
____________________ ERROR at setup of test_setup_fails ____________________

    @pytest.fixture
    def other():
>       assert 0
E       assert 0

test_module.py:7: AssertionError
================================= FAILURES =================================
_____________________________ test_call_fails ______________________________

something = None

    def test_call_fails(something):
>       assert 0
E       assert 0
```

### Add command line option with type checker and UsageError

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/example/simple.rst

Defines a conftest.py that adds a --cmdopt option with a custom type checker. The type_checker raises pytest.UsageError for invalid values, ensuring the option must start with 'type' followed by digits.

```python
# content of conftest.py
import pytest


def type_checker(value):
    msg = "cmdopt must specify a numeric type as typeNNN"
    if not value.startswith("type"):
        raise pytest.UsageError(msg)
    try:
        int(value[4:])
    except ValueError:
        raise pytest.UsageError(msg)

    return value


def pytest_addoption(parser):
    parser.addoption(
        "--cmdopt",
        action="store",
        default="type1",
        help="my option: type1 or type2",
        type=type_checker,
    )
```

### Define pytest_markeval_namespace hook in conftest.py

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/changelog.rst

Adds a custom namespace for evaluating skipif/xfail/xpass markers. The hook must return a dictionary, which augments the global variables available to marker expressions. This example returns a color variable set to 'red'.

```python
def pytest_markeval_namespace():
    return {"color": "red"}
```

### pytest --no-header output for test_verbosity_example.py

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/output.rst

Output of running pytest --no-header on the sample test file, showing the test session summary and failure details for the three failing tests.

```pytest
$ pytest --no-header
=========================== test session starts ============================
collected 4 items

test_verbosity_example.py .FFF                                       [100%]

================================= FAILURES =================================
_____________________________ test_words_fail ______________________________

    def test_words_fail():
        fruits1 = ["banana", "apple", "grapes", "melon", "kiwi"]
        fruits2 = ["banana", "apple", "orange", "melon", "kiwi"]
```

### Using autouse fixtures (autouse=True)

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/fixtures.rst

Demonstrates an autouse fixture (append_first) that is automatically applied to all tests without being explicitly requested. Both tests are affected by the autouse fixture, even though they don't request it.

```python
# contents of test_append.py
import pytest


@pytest.fixture
def first_entry():
    return "a"


@pytest.fixture
def order(first_entry):
    return []


@pytest.fixture(autouse=True)
def append_first(order, first_entry):
    return order.append(first_entry)



def test_string_only(order, first_entry):
    assert order == [first_entry]



def test_string_and_int(order, first_entry):
    order.append(2)
    assert order == [first_entry, 2]
```

### Skip test conditionally with pytest.mark.skipif

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/changelog.rst

Example from the changelog showing how to use @pytest.mark.skipif to skip a test when a condition is true. The condition uses the string 'color == "blue"' and the reason is 'Color is not red'.

```python
@pytest.mark.skipif("color == 'blue'", reason="Color is not red")
def test_func():
    assert False
```

### Decorate a funcarg in a test module

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/example/attic.rst

Extends the acceptance fixture in a test module by calling the next factory and creating a special directory in the tempdir. The test then checks that the special directory exists.

```python
def pytest_funcarg__accept(request):
    # call the next factory (living in our conftest.py)
    arg = request.getfuncargvalue("accept")
    # create a special layout in our tempdir
    arg.tmpdir.mkdir("special")
    return arg


class TestSpecialAcceptance:
    def test_sometest(self, accept):
        assert accept.tmpdir.join("special").check()
```

```toml
filterwarnings = [
    'error',
    'ignore::DeprecationWarning',
    # Note the use of single quote below to denote "raw" strings in TOML.
    'ignore:function ham\(\) should not be used:UserWarning',
]
```

--------------------------------

### Modular monkeypatch fixtures in test_app.py

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/monkeypatch.rst

Shows how to define separate fixtures for mocking configuration items using monkeypatch.setitem and monkeypatch.delitem, and how to reference them in tests.

```python
# contents of test_app.py
    import pytest

    # app.py with the connection string function
    import app


    # all of the mocks are moved into separated fixtures
    @pytest.fixture
    def mock_test_user(monkeypatch):
        """Set the DEFAULT_CONFIG user to test_user."""
        monkeypatch.setitem(app.DEFAULT_CONFIG, "user", "test_user")


    @pytest.fixture
    def mock_test_database(monkeypatch):
        """Set the DEFAULT_CONFIG database to test_db."""
        monkeypatch.setitem(app.DEFAULT_CONFIG, "database", "test_db")


    @pytest.fixture
    def mock_missing_default_user(monkeypatch):
        """Remove the user key from DEFAULT_CONFIG"""
        monkeypatch.delitem(app.DEFAULT_CONFIG, "user", raising=False)


    # tests reference only the fixture mocks that are needed
    def test_connection(mock_test_user, mock_test_database):
        expected = "User Id=test_user; Location=test_db;"

        result = app.create_connection_string()
        assert result == expected


    def test_missing_user(mock_missing_default_user):
        with pytest.raises(KeyError):
            _ = app.create_connection_string()
```

### Replace pytest_cmdline_preparse with pytest_load_initial_conftests

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/deprecations.rst

Shows the deprecated pytest_cmdline_preparse hook and its replacement, pytest_load_initial_conftests, with the new signature.

```python
def pytest_cmdline_preparse(config: Config, args: List[str]) -> None: ...


    # becomes:


    def pytest_load_initial_conftests(
        early_config: Config, parser: Parser, args: List[str]
    ) -> None: ...
```

### Hide traceback frames with __tracebackhide__

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/example/simple.rst

Set __tracebackhide__ = True in a helper function to hide its frame from pytest tracebacks unless --full-trace is used. The example shows a checkconfig function that fails when an object lacks a config attribute.

```python
def checkconfig(x):
    __tracebackhide__ = True
    if not hasattr(x, "config"):
        pytest.fail(f"not configured: {x}")


def test_something():
    checkconfig(42)
```

### Fixed code: convert generator to list in @pytest.mark.parametrize

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/deprecations.rst

Deprecated in 9.1. Fix the issue by converting generators and iterators to lists or tuples. This example uses list(data_generator()) so both tests run. range objects are Collection and are not affected.

```python
import pytest


def data_generator():
    yield 1
    yield 2


@pytest.mark.parametrize("n", list(data_generator()))
class Test:
    def test_1(self, n):
        pass

    def test_2(self, n):
        pass
```

### Inspect PYTEST_CURRENT_TEST with psutil

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/example/simple.rst

Use this snippet to inspect the PYTEST_CURRENT_TEST environment variable of running pytest processes, which can help identify a stuck test when console output is unavailable. It requires the psutil library and prints the process ID and the current test nodeid and stage (setup, call, or teardown).

```python
import psutil

for pid in psutil.pids():
    environ = psutil.Process(pid).environ()
    if "PYTEST_CURRENT_TEST" in environ:
        print(f'pytest process {pid} running: {environ["PYTEST_CURRENT_TEST"]}')
```

### Using string condition in pytest.mark.skipif

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/historical-notes.rst

Old way to specify skipif conditions as strings. The condition is evaluated by calling eval('sys.version_info >= (3,0)', namespace) during test function setup. The namespace contains all module globals, and os and sys as a minimum. String conditions remain fully supported.

```python
import sys


@pytest.mark.skipif("sys.version_info >= (3,3)")
def test_function(): ...
```

--------------------------------

### Session-scoped fixture using tmp_path_factory

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/tmp_path.rst

Shows how to use the session-scoped tmp_path_factory fixture to create a temporary directory and file once per session, saving time when generating expensive resources. The fixture is defined in conftest.py and used in a test.

```python
# contents of conftest.py
import pytest


@pytest.fixture(scope="session")
def image_file(tmp_path_factory):
    img = compute_expensive_image()
    fn = tmp_path_factory.mktemp("data") / "img.png"
    img.save(fn)
    return fn


# contents of test_image.py
def test_histogram(image_file):
    img = load_image(image_file)
    # compute and test histogram
```

--------------------------------

### pytest_generate_tests with class-level params

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/example/parametrize.rst

Defines a custom parametrization hook that reads argument sets from a class-level 'params' dictionary. Called once per test function; uses metafunc.parametrize to generate test cases. The example includes the TestClass with two test methods and their parameter sets.

```python
    def pytest_generate_tests(metafunc):
        # called once per each test function
        funcarglist = metafunc.cls.params[metafunc.function.__name__]
        argnames = sorted(funcarglist[0])
        metafunc.parametrize(
            argnames, [[funcargs[name] for name in argnames] for funcargs in funcarglist]
        )


    class TestClass:
        # a map specifying multiple argument sets for a test method
        params = {
            "test_equals": [dict(a=1, b=2), dict(a=3, b=3)],
            "test_zerodivision": [dict(a=1, b=0)],
        }

        def test_equals(self, a, b):
            assert a == b

        def test_zerodivision(self, a, b):
            with pytest.raises(ZeroDivisionError):
                a / b
```

### pyproject.toml entry point for pytest plugin

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/writing_plugins.rst

Define the pytest11 entry point in pyproject.toml to make your plugin discoverable by pytest. Include the 'Framework :: Pytest' classifier for PyPI. Confirm registration with 'pytest --trace-config'.

```toml
# sample ./pyproject.toml file
[build-system]
requires = ["hatchling"]
build-backend = "hatchling.build"

[project]
name = "myproject"
classifiers = [
    "Framework :: Pytest",
]

[project.entry-points.pytest11]
myproject = "myproject.pluginmodule"
```

### Before: class-scoped fixture as instance method (deprecated)

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/deprecations.rst

Deprecated in 9.1. Defining a class-scoped fixture as an instance method (without @classmethod) is deprecated. Attributes set on self are not visible to test methods because pytest creates a new instance per test method. This example shows the AttributeError that occurs.

```python
class TestExample:
    @pytest.fixture(scope="class")
    def setup_data(self):
        self.data = [1, 2, 3]  # This won't be visible to tests!

    def test_something(self, setup_data):
        assert self.data == [
            1,
            2,
            3,
        ]  # AttributeError: 'TestExample' object has no attribute 'data'
```

--------------------------------

### Configure hook with pytest.hookimpl(tryfirst=True)

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/deprecations.rst

Recommended replacement for configuring hook specs/impls. Use pytest.hookimpl decorator with attributes like tryfirst, trylast, optionalhook, and hookwrapper.

```python
@pytest.hookimpl(tryfirst=True)
def pytest_runtest_call(): ...
```
