### Configure pytest with ini-file options

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/announce/release-2.0.0.rst

Configure pytest using an ini-file (setup.cfg or tox.ini). The norecursedirs option prevents recursion into specified directories, and addopts adds default command-line options.

```ini
[pytest]
norecursedirs = .hg data*  # don't ever recurse in such dirs
addopts = -x --pyargs      # add these command line options by default
```

### Per-directory fixture scope with conftest.py

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/example/simple.rst

These files demonstrate per-directory fixture scopes using a conftest.py in directory 'a' that defines a package-scoped 'db' fixture. The two test modules in 'a' see the same fixture instance, while the test in sister directory 'b' does not see it and will error with 'fixture 'db' not found'.

```python
# content of a/conftest.py
import pytest


class DB:
    pass


@pytest.fixture(scope="package")
def db():
    return DB()
```

```python
# content of a/test_db.py
def test_a1(db):
    assert 0, db  # to show value
```

```python
# content of a/test_db2.py
def test_a2(db):
    assert 0, db  # to show value
```

```python
# content of b/test_error.py
def test_root(db):  # no db here, will error out
    pass
```

### Disable test ID escaping for unicode parametrization

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/parametrize.rst

Configuration option to disable escaping of non-ASCII characters in parametrization IDs. Use in pyproject.toml (TOML) or pytest.ini (INI). Enabling this may cause unwanted side effects or bugs depending on the OS and installed plugins; use at your own risk.

```toml
[pytest]
disable_test_id_escaping_and_forfeit_all_rights_to_community_support = true
```

```ini
[pytest]
disable_test_id_escaping_and_forfeit_all_rights_to_community_support = true
```

### pytest --help output

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/reference/reference.rst

Shows the complete help message and configuration info when running pytest --help. The output includes usage, positional arguments, and all command-line flags.

```bash

### Create and run tests with pytester.makepyfile and assert outcomes

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/writing_plugins.rst

Uses pytester.makepyfile to create a temporary test file with two tests, runs pytest with pytester.runpytest(), and asserts that all 4 tests passed. Note that assert_outcomes parses pytest's standard terminal summary; plugins that change or remove that summary can cause parsing failures, so disable them with -p no:<plugin> or set PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1.

```python
        # create a temporary pytest test file
        pytester.makepyfile(
            """
            def test_hello_default(hello):
                assert hello() == "Hello World!"

            def test_hello_name(hello, name):
                assert hello(name) == "Hello {0}!".format(name)
        """
        )

        # run all tests with pytest
        result = pytester.runpytest()

        # check that all 4 tests passed
        result.assert_outcomes(passed=4)
```

### Stacking parametrize decorators for combinations

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/parametrize.rst

Shows how to stack @pytest.mark.parametrize decorators to run a test with all combinations of parameters. The test runs with x=0/y=2, x=1/y=2, x=0/y=3, and x=1/y=3.

```python
import pytest


@pytest.mark.parametrize("x", [0, 1])
@pytest.mark.parametrize("y", [2, 3])
def test_foo(x, y):
    pass
```

--------------------------------

### Combined scope and params in fixture

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/funcarg_compare.rst

Combines session scope with parametrization. The factory runs twice per session, creating MySQL or PG based on request.param, and registers a finalizer to destroy the resource.

```python
@pytest.fixture(scope="session", params=["mysql", "pg"])
def db(request):
    if request.param == "mysql":
        db = MySQL()
    elif request.param == "pg":
        db = PG()
    request.addfinalizer(db.destroy)  # destroy when session is finished
    return db
```

### Requesting multiple fixtures in a test

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/fixtures.rst

Demonstrates a test and fixture requesting multiple fixtures at once. The 'order' fixture depends on 'first_entry' and 'second_entry', and the test 'test_string' requests both 'order' and 'expected_list'.

```python
# contents of test_append.py
import pytest


# Arrange
@pytest.fixture
def first_entry():
    return "a"


# Arrange
@pytest.fixture
def second_entry():
    return 2


# Arrange
@pytest.fixture
def order(first_entry, second_entry):
    return [first_entry, second_entry]


# Arrange
@pytest.fixture
def expected_list():
    return ["a", 2, 3.0]



def test_string(order, expected_list):
    # Act
    order.append(3.0)

    # Assert
    assert order == expected_list
```

### Fixture requesting another fixture

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/fixtures.rst

Demonstrates that fixtures can request other fixtures: the 'order' fixture depends on 'first_entry', and the test requests 'order'.

def first_entry():
    return "a"

@pytest.fixture
def order(first_entry):
    return [first_entry]

    # Assert

```

### Freeze pytest runner with app_main.py

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/example/simple.rst

Use this snippet to make a frozen application act as the pytest runner by handling the --pytest argument at startup. It explicitly imports third-party plugins (e.g., pytest_timeout) because entry-point plugin discovery does not work with frozen executables, and passes them to pytest.main.

```python
# contents of app_main.py
import sys

import pytest_timeout  # Third party plugin

if len(sys.argv) > 1 and sys.argv[1] == "--pytest":
    import pytest

    sys.exit(pytest.main(sys.argv[2:], plugins=[pytest_timeout]))
else:
    # normal application execution: at this point argv can be parsed
    # by your argument-parsing library of choice as usual
    ...
```

--------------------------------

### conftest.py plugin with custom marker and option

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/example/markers.rst

Defines a pytest plugin in conftest.py that adds a command line option '-E' and registers a custom marker 'env'. The pytest_runtest_setup hook skips tests if the environment specified by '-E' does not match the marker's argument.

```python
# content of conftest.py

import pytest


def pytest_addoption(parser):
    parser.addoption(
        "-E",
        action="store",
        metavar="NAME",
        help="only run tests matching the environment NAME.",
    )


def pytest_configure(config):
    # register an additional marker
    config.addinivalue_line(
        "markers", "env(name): mark test to run only on named environment"
    )


def pytest_runtest_setup(item):
    envnames = [mark.args[0] for mark in item.iter_markers(name="env")]
    if envnames:
        if item.config.getoption("-E") not in envnames:
            pytest.skip(f"test requires env in {envnames!r}")
```

# content of test_approx.py

### conftest.py: incremental marker with pytest_runtest_makereport and pytest_runtest_setup

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/example/simple.rst

Defines an 'incremental' marker in conftest.py. The two hooks record failures per class and parametrize index, then mark subsequent tests in the same class as xfail if a previous test failed.

```python
# content of conftest.py

import pytest

# store history of failures per test class name and per index in parametrize (if parametrize used)
_test_failed_incremental: dict[str, dict[tuple[int, ...], str]] = {}


def pytest_runtest_makereport(item, call):
    if item.get_closest_marker("incremental"):
        # incremental marker is used
        if call.excinfo is not None:
            # the test has failed
            # retrieve the class name of the test
            cls_name = str(item.cls)
            # retrieve the index of the test (if parametrize is used in combination with incremental)
            parametrize_index = (
                tuple(item.callspec.indices.values())
                if hasattr(item, "callspec")
                else ()
            )
            # retrieve the name of the test function
            test_name = item.originalname or item.name
            # store in _test_failed_incremental the original name of the failed test
            _test_failed_incremental.setdefault(cls_name, {}).setdefault(
                parametrize_index, test_name
            )


def pytest_runtest_setup(item):
    if item.get_closest_marker("incremental"):
        # retrieve the class name of the test
        cls_name = str(item.cls)
        # check if a previous test has failed for this class
        if cls_name in _test_failed_incremental:
            # retrieve the index of the test (if parametrize is used in combination with incremental)
            parametrize_index = (
                tuple(item.callspec.indices.values())
                if hasattr(item, "callspec")
                else ()
            )
            # retrieve the name of the first test function to fail for this class name and index
            test_name = _test_failed_incremental[cls_name].get(parametrize_index, None)
            # if name found, test has failed for the combination of class name & test name
            if test_name is not None:
                pytest.xfail(f"previous test failed ({test_name})")
```

### Reading config values: config.getini() instead of config.inicfg

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/deprecations.rst

Deprecated in 9.0. Direct access to config.inicfg is deprecated; use config.getini() to read configuration values. The deprecated and recommended usages are shown side by side.

```python
# Deprecated
value = config.inicfg["some_option"]

# Use this instead
value = config.getini("some_option")
```

### Custom assertion explanation with pytest_assertrepr_compare hook

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/assert.rst

Implements the pytest_assertrepr_compare hook in conftest.py to provide a custom explanation for Foo objects. The test module defines Foo and a failing comparison; running pytest -q shows the custom output.

```python
# content of conftest.py
   from test_foocompare import Foo


   def pytest_assertrepr_compare(op, left, right):
       if isinstance(left, Foo) and isinstance(right, Foo) and op == "==":
           return [
               "Comparing Foo instances:",
               f"   vals: {left.val} != {right.val}",
           ]
```

```python
# content of test_foocompare.py
   class Foo:
       def __init__(self, val):
           self.val = val

       def __eq__(self, other):
           return self.val == other.val


   def test_compare():
       f1 = Foo(1)
       f2 = Foo(2)
       assert f1 == f2
```

```bash
$ pytest -q test_foocompare.py
   F                                                                    [100%]
   ================================= FAILURES =================================
   _______________________________ test_compare _______________________________

       def test_compare():
           f1 = Foo(1)
           f2 = Foo(2)
   >       assert f1 == f2
   E       assert Comparing Foo instances:
   E            vals: 1 != 2

   test_foocompare.py:12: AssertionError
   ========================= short test summary info ==========================
   FAILED test_foocompare.py::test_compare - assert Comparing Foo instances:
   1 failed in 0.12s
```

### Read multiple markers applied to a test

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/example/markers.rst

Demonstrates applying the same marker multiple times and retrieving all instances using item.iter_markers in a conftest.py file.

```python
# content of test_mark_three_times.py
import pytest

pytestmark = pytest.mark.glob("module", x=1)


@pytest.mark.glob("class", x=2)
class TestClass:
    @pytest.mark.glob("function", x=3)
    def test_something(self):
        pass
```

```python
# content of conftest.py
import sys


def pytest_runtest_setup(item):
    for mark in item.iter_markers(name="glob"):
        print(f"glob args={mark.args} kwargs={mark.kwargs}")
        sys.stdout.flush()
```

```pytest
$ pytest -q -s

glob args=('module',) kwargs={'x': 1}
.
1 passed in 0.12s

### Factory as fixture returning data

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/fixtures.rst

The factory-as-fixture pattern returns a function that generates data. The test calls the factory multiple times to create distinct records.

```python
@pytest.fixture
def make_customer_record():
    def _make_customer_record(name):
        return {"name": name, "orders": []}

    return _make_customer_record


def test_customer_records(make_customer_record):
    customer_1 = make_customer_record("Lisa")
    customer_2 = make_customer_record("Mike")
    customer_3 = make_customer_record("Meredith")

```

--------------------------------

### Monkeypatch Path.home to return a fixed path

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/monkeypatch.rst

Use monkeypatch.setattr to replace Path.home with a mock function that always returns Path('/abc'), removing dependency on the running user. The patch must be applied before the function using it is called, and is undone after the test finishes.

```python
# contents of test_module.py with source code and the test
from pathlib import Path


def getssh():
    """Simple function to return expanded homedir ssh path."""
    return Path.home() / ".ssh"


def test_getssh(monkeypatch):
    # mocked return function to replace Path.home
    # always return '/abc'
    def mockreturn():
        return Path("/abc")

    # Application of the monkeypatch to replace Path.home
    # with the behavior of mockreturn defined above.
    monkeypatch.setattr(Path, "home", mockreturn)
```

--------------------------------

### New-style fixture with direct scope and finalizer

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/funcarg_compare.rst

Uses @pytest.fixture(scope="session") to create a Database once per session and registers a finalizer with request.addfinalizer(). No need for cached_setup().

```python
@pytest.fixture(scope="session")
def db(request):
    # factory will only be invoked once per session -
    db = DataBase()
    request.addfinalizer(db.destroy)  # destroy when session is finished
    return db
```

--------------------------------

### conftest.py for YAML test collection

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/example/nonpython.rst

This conftest.py collects test*.yaml files and executes the YAML content as custom tests. It defines a YamlItem class with runtest, repr_failure, and reportinfo methods. The repr_failure method is called for representing test failures, and reportinfo returns a tuple (path, lineno, description) for test location.

```python
# content of conftest.py
import pytest

def pytest_collect_file(parent, path):
    if path.ext == ".yaml" and path.basename.startswith("test"):
        return YamlFile.from_parent(parent, path=path)

class YamlFile(pytest.File):
    def collect(self):
        import yaml

        raw = yaml.safe_load(self.path.open())
        for name, spec in sorted(raw.items()):
            yield YamlItem.from_parent(self, name=name, spec=spec)

class YamlItem(pytest.Item):
    def __init__(self, *, name, parent, spec):
        super().__init__(name, parent)
        self.spec = spec

    def runtest(self):
        for name, value in self.spec.items():
            if name != value:
                raise YamlFailure(self, name, value)

    def repr_failure(self, excinfo):
        """Called when self.runtest() raises an exception."""
        if isinstance(excinfo.value, YamlFailure):
            return "\n".join(
                [
                    "usecase execution failed",
                    "   spec failed: {!r}: {!r}".format(
                        excinfo.value.name, excinfo.value.value
                    ),
                    "   no further details known at this point.",
                ]
            )

    def reportinfo(self):
        return self.path, 0, f"usecase: {self.name}"

class YamlFailure(Exception):
    def __init__(self, item, name, value):
        self.item = item
        self.name = name
        self.value = value
```

```

### Mock returned objects with monkeypatch.setattr and a custom class

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/monkeypatch.rst

Shows how to mock a returned object from a function by defining a custom class with the required methods and using monkeypatch.setattr to replace the function. The first block is the application code that makes an HTTP request; the second block is the test that patches requests.get with a mock returning a MockResponse instance.

```python
# contents of app.py, a simple API retrieval example
import requests


def get_json(url):
    """Takes a URL, and returns the JSON."""
    r = requests.get(url)
    return r.json()
```

```python
# contents of test_app.py, a simple test for our API retrieval
# import requests for the purposes of monkeypatching
import requests

# our app.py that includes the get_json() function
# this is the previous code block example
import app


# custom class to be the mock return value
# will override the requests.Response returned from requests.get
class MockResponse:
    # mock json() method always returns a specific testing dictionary
    @staticmethod
    def json():
        return {"mock_key": "mock_response"}


def test_get_json(monkeypatch):
    # Any arguments may be passed and mock_get() will always return our
    # mocked object, which only has the .json() method.
    def mock_get(*args, **kwargs):
        return MockResponse()

    # apply the monkeypatch for requests.get to mock_get
    monkeypatch.setattr(requests, "get", mock_get)

    # app.get_json, which contains requests.get, uses the monkeypatch
    result = app.get_json("https://fakeurl")
    assert result["mock_key"] == "mock_response"
```

### Set custom truncation limits (truncation_limit_lines, truncation_limit_chars)

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/output.rst

Configure custom truncation limits for assertion output using the truncation_limit_lines and truncation_limit_chars options. The default limits are 8 lines or 640 characters, whichever comes first. Setting both to 0 disables truncation; setting only one disables that mode while leaving the other intact.

```toml
[pytest]
truncation_limit_lines = 10
truncation_limit_chars = 90
```

```ini
[pytest]
truncation_limit_lines = 10
truncation_limit_chars = 90
```

### Functional test with fixtures for cookiecutter project generation

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/proposals/parametrize_with_fixtures.rst

Shows a functional test that generates a project using cookiecutter with default and extra context fixtures. The extra_context fixture is parametrized with different author and project_slug values, and the context fixture selects between default and extra contexts using request.getfuncargvalue(). This approach has issues with parametrized fixtures.

```python
import pytest


@pytest.fixture
def default_context():
    return {"extra_context": {}}


@pytest.fixture(
    params=[
        {"author": "alice"},
        {"project_slug": "helloworld"},
        {"author": "bob", "project_slug": "foobar"},
    ]
)
def extra_context(request):
    return {"extra_context": request.param}


@pytest.fixture(params=["default", "extra"])
def context(request):
    if request.param == "default":
        return request.getfuncargvalue("default_context")
    else:
        return request.getfuncargvalue("extra_context")


def test_generate_project(cookies, context):
    """Call the cookiecutter API to generate a new project from a
    template.
    """
    result = cookies.bake(extra_context=context)

    assert result.exit_code == 0
    assert result.exception is None
    assert result.project.isdir()

```

### Parametrize fixture with params and request.param

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/fixtures.rst

Defines a parametrized fixture with two SMTP server parameters. The fixture yields an SMTP connection and finalizes it after tests complete.

```python
# content of conftest.py
import smtplib

import pytest


@pytest.fixture(scope="module", params=["smtp.gmail.com", "mail.python.org"])
def smtp_connection(request):
    smtp_connection = smtplib.SMTP(request.param, 587, timeout=5)
    yield smtp_connection
    print(f"finalizing {smtp_connection}")
    smtp_connection.close()
```

### Use a fixture from another fixture to build an App object

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/fixtures.rst

Demonstrates modular fixtures: an `app` fixture (scope="module") receives the previously defined `smtp_connection` fixture and instantiates an `App` object. Running `pytest -v test_appsetup.py` passes both parametrized invocations.

```python
# content of test_appsetup.py

import pytest


class App:
    def __init__(self, smtp_connection):
        self.smtp_connection = smtp_connection


@pytest.fixture(scope="module")
def app(smtp_connection):
    return App(smtp_connection)


def test_smtp_connection_exists(app):
    assert app.smtp_connection
```

```pytest

============================ 2 passed in 0.12s =============================

### Implement pytest_my_hook to print hooks

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/writing_hook_functions.rst

Implements the pytest_my_hook hook in a conftest.py file to print all active hooks. The function must have the correct signature and be defined in a conftest.py or external plugin.

```python
def pytest_my_hook(config):
    """
    Print all active hooks to the screen.
    """
    print(config.hook)

### conftest.py - generate parameters from command line

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/example/parametrize.rst

Adds a --all option and uses pytest_generate_tests to parametrize 'param1' with a range of values. Without --all, it runs 2 tests; with --all, it runs 5 tests, causing the last one to fail.

```python
# content of conftest.py


def pytest_addoption(parser):
    parser.addoption("--all", action="store_true", help="run all combinations")


def pytest_generate_tests(metafunc):
    if "param1" in metafunc.fixturenames:
        if metafunc.config.getoption("all"):
            end = 5
        else:
            end = 2
        metafunc.parametrize("param1", range(end))
```

--------------------------------

### Using request.addfinalizer for teardown in fixtures

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/fixtures.rst

Shows how to use request.addfinalizer to register teardown callbacks in fixtures. The finalizer is added only after the fixture has done something that needs cleanup, and it runs even if the fixture raises an exception after registration. The output shows the test passing.

```python
# content of test_emaillib.py
from emaillib import Email, MailAdminClient

import pytest


@pytest.fixture
def mail_admin():
    return MailAdminClient()


@pytest.fixture
def sending_user(mail_admin):
    user = mail_admin.create_user()
    yield user
    mail_admin.delete_user(user)


@pytest.fixture
def receiving_user(mail_admin, request):
    user = mail_admin.create_user()

    def delete_user():
        mail_admin.delete_user(user)

    request.addfinalizer(delete_user)
    return user


@pytest.fixture
def email(sending_user, receiving_user, request):
    _email = Email(subject="Hey!", body="How's it going?")
    sending_user.send_email(_email, receiving_user)

    def empty_mailbox():
        receiving_user.clear_mailbox()

    request.addfinalizer(empty_mailbox)
    return _email


def test_email_received(receiving_user, email):
    assert email in receiving_user.inbox

```

```pytest
$ pytest -q test_emaillib.py
.                                                                    [100%]
1 passed in 0.12s

```

--------------------------------

### Package layout and pytest invocation for prepend/append import modes

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/explanation/pythonpath.rst

Shows a package layout where test modules and conftest.py files are inside packages. When running 'pytest root/', pytest inserts 'root/' at the front of sys.path to import test_foo.py as the module 'foo.bar.tests.test_foo' and conftest.py as 'foo.conftest'.

```text
root/
|- foo/
   |- __init__.py
   |- conftest.py
   |- bar/
      |- __init__.py
      |- tests/
         |- __init__.py
         |- test_foo.py

```

```bash
pytest root/
```

--------------------------------

### Call a hook from a fixture with pytestconfig.hook

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/writing_hook_functions.rst

Calls a hook from a fixture using the pytestconfig fixture. Hooks receive parameters only as keyword arguments, and the result is a list of return values from all registered functions.

```python
@pytest.fixture()
def my_fixture(pytestconfig):
    # call the hook called "pytest_my_hook"
    # 'result' will be a list of return values from all registered functions.
    result = pytestconfig.hook.pytest_my_hook(config=pytestconfig)
```

### Show extra info on xfailed, xpassed, and skipped tests with pytest -rxXs

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/skipping.rst

Use the -r option with the short letters 'x', 'X', and 's' to display detailed information about xfailed, xpassed, and skipped tests in the test summary. This is a command-line invocation, not a Python code snippet.

```bash
pytest -rxXs  # show extra info on xfailed, xpassed, and skipped tests
```

### Boolean condition with request.config.getoption

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/historical-notes.rst

Equivalent boolean condition using request.config to skip if the --db option is not set. Uses an autouse fixture and pytest.skip.

```python
@pytest.fixture(autouse=True)
def skip_if_no_db(request):
    if not request.config.getoption("--db", default=False):
        pytest.skip("--db was not specified")


def test_function():
    pass
```

### Define autouse fixture in unittest TestCase to use tmp_path

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/unittest.rst

Python code for a unittest TestCase with an autouse fixture that changes to a temporary directory and creates a samplefile.ini. The fixture uses pytest's tmp_path and monkeypatch fixtures, and the test reads the file to verify its content.

```python
# content of test_unittest_cleandir.py
import unittest

import pytest


class MyTest(unittest.TestCase):
    @pytest.fixture(autouse=True)
    def initdir(self, tmp_path, monkeypatch):
        monkeypatch.chdir(tmp_path)  # change to pytest-provided temporary directory
        tmp_path.joinpath("samplefile.ini").write_text("# testdata", encoding="utf-8")

    def test_method(self):
        with open("samplefile.ini", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            s = f.read()
        assert "testdata" in s
```

### Inject namespace with doctest_namespace fixture

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/doctest.rst

Inject items into the doctest namespace using the doctest_namespace fixture. The first block defines an autouse fixture in conftest.py that adds numpy as np; the second block shows a doctest in numpy.py that uses the injected np. The conftest.py must be in the same directory tree as the doctest.

```python
# content of conftest.py
import pytest
import numpy


@pytest.fixture(autouse=True)
def add_np(doctest_namespace):
    doctest_namespace["np"] = numpy
```

```python
# content of numpy.py
def arange():
    """
    >>> a = np.arange(10)
    >>> len(a)
    10
    """
```

### Set addopts in pytest.toml

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/example/pythoncollection.rst

Adds --pyargs to the default options, so a simple 'pytest NAME' invocation will check if NAME exists as an importable package/module and otherwise treat it as a filesystem path.

```toml
# content of pytest.toml
[pytest]
addopts = ["--pyargs"]
```

### Patching dictionary values with monkeypatch.setitem - test_app.py

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/monkeypatch.rst

Uses monkeypatch.setitem to temporarily change the user and database values in app.DEFAULT_CONFIG for a single test, then verifies that create_connection_string produces the expected string.

```python
# contents of test_app.py
# app.py with the connection string function (prior code block)
import app


def test_connection(monkeypatch):
    # Patch the values of DEFAULT_CONFIG to specific
    # testing values only for this test.
    monkeypatch.setitem(app.DEFAULT_CONFIG, "user", "test_user")
    monkeypatch.setitem(app.DEFAULT_CONFIG, "database", "test_db")

    # expected result based on the mocks
    expected = "User Id=test_user; Location=test_db;"

    # the test uses the monkeypatched dictionary settings
    result = app.create_connection_string()
    assert result == expected
```

--------------------------------

### List available markers with pytest --markers

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/example/markers.rst

Use the --markers command to display all registered markers, including custom ones and built-in pytest markers.

```pytest
$ pytest --markers
@pytest.mark.webtest: mark a test as a webtest.

@pytest.mark.slow: mark test as slow.

@pytest.mark.filterwarnings(warning): add a warning filter to the given test. see https://docs.pytest.org/en/stable/how-to/capture-warnings.html#pytest-mark-filterwarnings

@pytest.mark.skip(reason=None): skip the given test function with an optional reason. Example: skip(reason="no way of currently testing this") skips the test.

@pytest.mark.skipif(condition, ..., *, reason=...): skip the given test function if any of the conditions evaluate to True. Example: skipif(sys.platform == 'win32') skips the test if we are on the win32 platform. See https://docs.pytest.org/en/stable/reference/reference.html#pytest-mark-skipif

@pytest.mark.xfail(condition, ..., *, reason=..., run=True, raises=None, strict=strict_xfail): mark the test function as an expected failure if any of the conditions evaluate to True. Optionally specify a reason for better reporting and run=False if you don't even want to execute the test function. If only specific exception(s) are expected, you can list them in raises, and if the test fails in other ways, it will be reported as a true failure. See https://docs.pytest.org/en/stable/reference/reference.html#pytest-mark-xfail

@pytest.mark.parametrize(argnames, argvalues): call a test function multiple times passing in different arguments in turn. argvalues generally needs to be a list of values if argnames specifies only one name or a list of tuples of values if argnames specifies multiple names. Example: @parametrize('arg1', [1,2]) would lead to two calls of the decorated test function, one with arg1=1 and another with arg1=2.see https://docs.pytest.org/en/stable/how-to/parametrize.html for more info and examples.

@pytest.mark.usefixtures(fixturename1, fixturename2, ...): mark tests as needing all of the specified fixtures. see https://docs.pytest.org/en/stable/explanation/fixtures.html#usefixtures

@pytest.mark.tryfirst: mark a hook implementation function such that the plugin machinery will try to call it first/as early as possible. DEPRECATED, use @pytest.hookimpl(tryfirst=True) instead.

@pytest.mark.trylast: mark a hook implementation function such that the plugin machinery will try to call it last/as late as possible. DEPRECATED, use @pytest.hookimpl(trylast=True) instead.
```

### Finalizer execution order (last added runs first)

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/fixtures.rst

Shows that finalizers added via request.addfinalizer run in reverse order of registration: the last added finalizer runs first. The output shows finalizer_1 before finalizer_2.

```python
# content of test_finalizers.py
from functools import partial
import pytest


@pytest.fixture
def fix_w_finalizers(request):
    request.addfinalizer(partial(print, "finalizer_2"))
    request.addfinalizer(partial(print, "finalizer_1"))


def test_bar(fix_w_finalizers):
    print("test_bar")

```

```pytest
$ pytest -s test_finalizers.py
=========================== test session starts ============================
platform linux -- Python 3.x.y, pytest-9.x.y, pluggy-1.x.y
rootdir: /home/sweet/project
collected 1 item

test_finalizers.py test_bar
.finalizer_1
finalizer_2


============================ 1 passed in 0.12s =============================

```

### Basic parametrize with xfail marker

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/parametrize.rst

Defines a test function with parametrized inputs and expected values. One parameter set is marked with pytest.mark.xfail to indicate it is expected to fail. Running pytest shows 2 passed and 1 xfailed.

```python
# content of test_expectation.py
import pytest


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    "test_input,expected",
    [("3+5", 8), ("2+4", 6), pytest.param("6*9", 42, marks=pytest.mark.xfail)],
)
def test_eval(test_input, expected):
    assert eval(test_input) == expected
```

```pytest
$ pytest
=========================== test session starts ============================
platform linux -- Python 3.x.y, pytest-9.x.y, pluggy-1.x.y
rootdir: /home/sweet/project
collected 3 items

test_expectation.py ..x                                              [100%]

======================= 2 passed, 1 xfailed in 0.12s =======================
```

### Fix: request fixture as parameter

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/deprecations.rst

Recommended replacement: request the fixture function directly as a parameter in the dependent fixture instead of calling it directly.

```python
@pytest.fixture
    def cell():
        return ...


    @pytest.fixture
    def full_cell(cell):
        cell.make_full()
        return cell
```

### Using pytest.RaisesExc for detailed exception matching

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/assert.rst

Shows how to use pytest.RaisesExc inside pytest.RaisesGroup to specify more details about the contained exception, such as a match parameter.

```python
def test_raises_exc():
    with pytest.RaisesGroup(pytest.RaisesExc(ValueError, match="foo")):
        raise ExceptionGroup("", (ValueError("foo")))
```

### Basic subtest usage with subtests.test

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/subtests.rst

Shows how to use the subtests fixture to group assertions within a test. Each iteration creates a subtest with a custom message and parameter i. Assertion failures are caught and reported individually.

```python
# content of test_subtest.py


def test(subtests):
    for i in range(5):
        with subtests.test(msg="custom message", i=i):
            assert i % 2 == 0
```

--------------------------------

### Alternative approach with pytest.fixture_request in fixture

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/proposals/parametrize_with_fixtures.rst

Shows an alternative helper function pytest.fixture_request used inside a parametrized fixture to yield all parameters from the referenced fixtures. The context fixture returns request.param, which will be each value from default_context and extra_context in order.

```python
@pytest.fixture(
    params=[
        pytest.fixture_request("default_context"),
        pytest.fixture_request("extra_context"),
    ]
)
def context(request):
    """Returns all values for ``default_context``, one-by-one before it
    does the same for ``extra_context``.

    request.param:
        - {}
        - {'author': 'alice'}
        - {'project_slug': 'helloworld'}
        - {'author': 'bob', 'project_slug': 'foobar'}
    """
    return request.param

```

--------------------------------

### Override a fixture at the directory (conftest) level

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/fixtures.rst

Shows a directory structure where a fixture named username is defined in tests/conftest.py and overridden in tests/subdir/conftest.py. The overriding fixture can access the base fixture by accepting it as an argument.

```text
tests/
    conftest.py
        # content of tests/conftest.py
        import pytest

        @pytest.fixture
        def username():
            return 'username'

    test_something.py
        # content of tests/test_something.py
        def test_username(username):
            assert username == 'username'

    subdir/
        conftest.py
            # content of tests/subdir/conftest.py
            import pytest

            @pytest.fixture
            def username(username):
                return 'overridden-' + username

        test_something_else.py
            # content of tests/subdir/test_something_else.py
            def test_username(username):
                assert username == 'overridden-username'
```

### Use py.test.raises as a context manager

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/changelog.rst

Use py.test.raises as a context manager to assert that a block raises a specific exception. The second form captures the exception info in excinfo for additional checks on value, type, or traceback.

```python
with py.test.raises(ZeroDivisionError):
    x = 0
    1 / x

with py.test.raises(RuntimeError) as excinfo:
    call_something()

# you may do extra checks on excinfo.value|type|traceback here
```

### Add command line option and fixture in conftest.py

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/example/simple.rst

This conftest.py defines a pytest_addoption hook to add a --cmdopt option with a default of type1, and a fixture that returns the option value. The fixture is used by the test function.

```python
# content of conftest.py
import pytest


def pytest_addoption(parser):
    parser.addoption(
        "--cmdopt", action="store", default="type1", help="my option: type1 or type2"
    )


@pytest.fixture
def cmdopt(request):
    return request.config.getoption("--cmdopt")
```

### Legacy namespace package with pkg_resources.declare_namespace()

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/deprecations.rst

Legacy namespace package using pkg_resources.declare_namespace() in __init__.py. This triggers a deprecation warning when pkg_resources is imported and the path contains a declared namespace package. Replace by removing the __init__.py file entirely to use native namespace packages (PEP 420).

```python
# mypkg/__init__.py
__import__("pkg_resources").declare_namespace(__name__)
```

--------------------------------

### Specify additional plugins to pytest.main()

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/usage.rst

Create a custom plugin class and pass it to pytest.main() via the plugins parameter. The plugin's hook is invoked during the test run.

```python
# content of myinvoke.py
import sys

import pytest


class MyPlugin:
    def pytest_sessionfinish(self):
        print("*** test run reporting finishing")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(pytest.main(["-qq"], plugins=[MyPlugin()]))
```

== 1 failed, 1 passed, 1 skipped, 1 xfailed, 1 xpassed, 1 error in 0.12s ===

--------------------------------

### Using flatten_subgroups and allow_unwrapped parameters

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/assert.rst

Shows how to use the flatten_subgroups and allow_unwrapped parameters of pytest.RaisesGroup to control strictness about structure and unwrapped exceptions.

```python
def test_structure():
    with pytest.RaisesGroup(pytest.RaisesGroup(ValueError)):
        raise ExceptionGroup("", (ExceptionGroup("", (ValueError(),)),))
    with pytest.RaisesGroup(ValueError, flatten_subgroups=True):
        raise ExceptionGroup("1st group", [ExceptionGroup("2nd group", [ValueError()])])
    with pytest.RaisesGroup(ValueError, allow_unwrapped=True):
        raise ValueError
```

### Register markers programmatically with pytest_configure

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/mark.rst

Use the pytest_configure hook to add a custom marker at runtime. This is an alternative to configuration file registration.

```python
def pytest_configure(config):
    config.addinivalue_line(
        "markers", "env(name): mark test to run only on named environment"
    )
```

### Add property with record_property fixture

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/output.rst

Uses the record_property fixture to add an extra property 'example_key' with value '1' to the generated testcase tag in JUnit XML output.

```python
def test_function(record_property):
    record_property("example_key", 1)
    assert True
```

--------------------------------

### Inspect warnings with pytest.warns record

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/capture-warnings.rst

Use pytest.warns() with the as record form to capture and inspect the raised warnings as WarningMessage objects.

```python
with pytest.warns(RuntimeWarning) as record:
    warnings.warn("another warning", RuntimeWarning)

# check that only one warning was raised
assert len(record) == 1
# check that the message matches
assert record[0].message.args[0] == "another warning"
```

```bash
register-python-argcomplete pytest >> ~/.bashrc
```

--------------------------------

### test_time.py - different options for test IDs

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/example/parametrize.rst

Shows four parametrized test functions (v0, v1, v2, v3) demonstrating different ways to specify test IDs: default generation, a list of strings, a function, and pytest.param with explicit ids.

```python
# content of test_time.py

from datetime import datetime, timedelta

import pytest

testdata = [
    (datetime(2001, 12, 12), datetime(2001, 12, 11), timedelta(1)),
    (datetime(2001, 12, 11), datetime(2001, 12, 12), timedelta(-1)),
]


@pytest.mark.parametrize("a,b,expected", testdata)
def test_timedistance_v0(a, b, expected):
    diff = a - b
    assert diff == expected


@pytest.mark.parametrize("a,b,expected", testdata, ids=["forward", "backward"])
def test_timedistance_v1(a, b, expected):
    diff = a - b
    assert diff == expected


def idfn(val):
    if isinstance(val, (datetime,)):
        # note this wouldn't show any hours/minutes/seconds
        return val.strftime("%Y%m%d")


@pytest.mark.parametrize("a,b,expected", testdata, ids=idfn)
def test_timedistance_v2(a, b, expected):
    diff = a - b
    assert diff == expected


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    "a,b,expected",
    [
        pytest.param(
            datetime(2001, 12, 12), datetime(2001, 12, 11), timedelta(1), id="forward"
        ),
        pytest.param(
            datetime(2001, 12, 11), datetime(2001, 12, 12), timedelta(-1), id="backward"
        ),
    ],
)
def test_timedistance_v3(a, b, expected):
    diff = a - b
    assert diff == expected
```

### Tag next dev version

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/RELEASING.rst

Tags the release cherry-pick merge commit in main with a dev tag for the next feature release. Only for major/minor releases or first prerelease.

```bash
git checkout main
git pull
git tag MAJOR.{MINOR+1}.0.dev0
git push upstream MAJOR.{MINOR+1}.0.dev0
```

Adds custom markers to the whitelist and enables strict mode via addopts to avoid future regressions.

```toml
[pytest]
addopts = ["--strict-markers"]
markers = ["slow", "serial"]
```

### pytest --last-failed --last-failed-no-failures all/none

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/cache.rst

Shows the two possible values for the --last-failed-no-failures option: 'all' (default) runs the full test suite when there are no known failures, while 'none' runs no tests and exits successfully.

```bash
pytest --last-failed --last-failed-no-failures all    # runs the full test suite (default behavior)
pytest --last-failed --last-failed-no-failures none   # runs no tests and exits successfully
```

### Deprecated hook configuration with pytest.mark.tryfirst

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/deprecations.rst

Old way of configuring hook specs/impls using pytest.mark.tryfirst. Deprecated since pytest 7.2; use pytest.hookimpl instead.

```python
@pytest.mark.tryfirst
def pytest_runtest_call(): ...


# or
def pytest_runtest_call(): ...


pytest_runtest_call.tryfirst = True
```

### Test function that reads a command line option via fixture

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/example/simple.rst

This Python test function uses a cmdopt fixture to receive a value from the command line. It prints different messages based on the value and then fails to show the captured output.

```python
# content of test_sample.py
def test_answer(cmdopt):
    if cmdopt == "type1":
        print("first")
    elif cmdopt == "type2":
        print("second")
    assert 0  # to see what was printed
```

### Add numpy to doctest namespace with autouse fixture

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/builtin.rst

Use an autouse fixture to add numpy to the doctest namespace so doctests can access it. The fixture must be named add_np and use the doctest_namespace fixture.

```python
@pytest.fixture(autouse=True)
def add_np(doctest_namespace):
    doctest_namespace["np"] = numpy
```

--------------------------------

### Module-scoped smtp_connection fixture in conftest.py

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/fixtures.rst

Defines a module-scoped fixture in conftest.py that creates an SMTP connection, and a test module that uses it in two tests. The fixture is invoked once per module, so both tests receive the same instance. The tests include assert 0 to demonstrate failure and show the shared fixture in the traceback.

```python
# content of conftest.py
import smtplib

import pytest


@pytest.fixture(scope="module")
def smtp_connection():
    return smtplib.SMTP("smtp.gmail.com", 587, timeout=5)

```

```python
# content of test_module.py


def test_ehlo(smtp_connection):
    response, msg = smtp_connection.ehlo()
    assert response == 250
    assert b"smtp.gmail.com" in msg
    assert 0  # for demo purposes


def test_noop(smtp_connection):
    response, msg = smtp_connection.noop()
    assert response == 250
    assert 0  # for demo purposes

```

--------------------------------

### Yield fixture teardown order (first-in-last-out)

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/fixtures.rst

Demonstrates that yield fixtures execute teardown code in first-in-last-out order: the right-most fixture's teardown runs first. The output shows after_yield_2 before after_yield_1.

```python
# content of test_finalizers.py
import pytest


def test_bar(fix_w_yield1, fix_w_yield2):
    print("test_bar")


@pytest.fixture
def fix_w_yield1():
    yield
    print("after_yield_1")


@pytest.fixture
def fix_w_yield2():
    yield
    print("after_yield_2")

```

```pytest
$ pytest -s test_finalizers.py
=========================== test session starts ============================
platform linux -- Python 3.x.y, pytest-9.x.y, pluggy-1.x.y
rootdir: /home/sweet/project
collected 1 item

test_finalizers.py test_bar
.after_yield_2
after_yield_1


============================ 1 passed in 0.12s =============================

```

### Setting config values: pytest -o command line option

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/deprecations.rst

--------------------------------

### Accessing exception information

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/assert.rst

Capture the exception info object using the 'as' keyword with pytest.raises to inspect the exception's attributes.

```python
def test_recursion_depth():
    with pytest.raises(RuntimeError) as excinfo:

        def f():
            f()

        f()
    assert "maximum recursion" in str(excinfo.value)
```

### Override parametrized fixture with non-parametrized and vice versa

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/fixtures.rst

Demonstrates overriding a parametrized fixture with a non-parametrized one and vice versa at the test module level. The conftest.py defines the original fixtures, test_something.py overrides them, and test_something_else.py uses the original ones.

```python
# content of tests/conftest.py
import pytest

@pytest.fixture(params=['one', 'two', 'three'])
def parametrized_username(request):
    return request.param

@pytest.fixture
def non_parametrized_username(request):
    return 'username'
```

```python
# content of tests/test_something.py
import pytest

@pytest.fixture
def parametrized_username():
    return 'overridden-username'

@pytest.fixture(params=['one', 'two', 'three'])
def non_parametrized_username(request):
    return request.param

def test_username(parametrized_username):
    assert parametrized_username == 'overridden-username'

def test_parametrized_username(non_parametrized_username):
    assert non_parametrized_username in ['one', 'two', 'three']
```

```python
# content of tests/test_something_else.py
def test_username(parametrized_username):
    assert parametrized_username in ['one', 'two', 'three']

def test_username(non_parametrized_username):
    assert non_parametrized_username == 'username'
```

### Ensure only certain warnings with recwarn

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/capture-warnings.rst

Use this to ensure only certain warnings are issued. It checks that exactly one warning was recorded and that it is a UserWarning.

```python
def test_warning(recwarn):
    ...
    assert len(recwarn) == 1
    user_warning = recwarn.pop(UserWarning)
    assert issubclass(user_warning.category, UserWarning)
```

--------------------------------

### Renaming rules from py.test to pytest

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/naming20.rst

Shows the mapping from old py.test namespace accesses to the new flat pytest module. The old py.test.* ways remain valid but global renaming is encouraged.

```text
py.test.XYZ          -> pytest.XYZ
py.test.collect.XYZ  -> pytest.XYZ
py.test.cmdline.main -> pytest.main
```

--------------------------------

### Marking parametrize parameters with pytest.mark.xfail (old syntax)

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/historical-notes.rst

Old syntax for marking parametrized values with pytest.mark.xfail. This was an initial hack, incomplete and broken for passing functions or applying multiple marks with the same name but different parameters. Planned to be removed in pytest-4.0.

```python
import pytest


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    "test_input,expected", [("3+5", 8), ("2+4", 6), pytest.mark.xfail(("6*9", 42))]
)
def test_eval(test_input, expected):
    assert eval(test_input) == expected
```

### Register hookspecs and use hook in pytest_addoption

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/writing_hook_functions.rst

Shows how to register custom hook specifications via pytest_addhooks and then call the hook in pytest_addoption to set a default value for a command-line option.

```python
# contents of myplugin.py


def pytest_addhooks(pluginmanager):
    """This example assumes the hooks are grouped in the 'hooks' module."""
    from . import hooks

    pluginmanager.add_hookspecs(hooks)


def pytest_addoption(parser, pluginmanager):
    default_value = pluginmanager.hook.pytest_config_file_default_value()
    parser.addoption(
        "--config-file",
        help="Config file to use, defaults to %(default)s",
        default=default_value,
    )
```

### Production function with type annotations

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/explanation/types.rst

Shows a function with type annotations to illustrate a bug that a type checker can catch but full coverage tests may miss.

```python
def get_caption(target: int, items: list[tuple[int, str]]) -> str:
    for value, caption in items:
        if value == target:
            return caption

```

### Alternative fix: fixture with name parameter

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/deprecations.rst

Alternative fix when the fixture function is called multiple times inside a test or minimal changes are desired: create a fixture that calls the original function and use the name parameter to expose it.

```python
def cell():
        return ...


    @pytest.fixture(name="cell")
    def cell_fixture():
        return cell()
```

### Set maximum warnings via command line

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/capture-warnings.rst

Use --max-warnings to fail the test run if the total number of warnings exceeds the threshold. Exit code 6 (MAX_WARNINGS_ERROR) is reported when all tests pass but the threshold is exceeded.

```bash
pytest --max-warnings=10
```

### Use parser.addini with int and float types

Shows how to use the new "int" and "float" types for parser.addini in a pytest_addoption hook, and the corresponding pytest.ini configuration file that sets those values.

```python
def pytest_addoption(parser):
    parser.addini("int_value", type="int", default=2, help="my int value")

### caplog

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/builtin.rst

Fixture that provides access to captured logs.

```APIDOC
## Fixture: caplog

### Description
Access and control log capturing. Captured logs are available through the following properties/methods:

- caplog.messages -> list of format-interpolated log messages
- caplog.text -> string containing formatted log output
- caplog.records -> list of logging.LogRecord instances
- caplog.record_tuples -> list of (logger_name, level, message) tuples
- caplog.clear() -> clear captured records and formatted log output string
```

### Disable plugin autoload and specify plugins manually

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/plugins.rst

Disable automatic plugin loading and manually specify plugins. The first block uses environment variables, the second uses the command-line option, and the last two show TOML and INI configuration alternatives. This feature was added in version 8.4.

```bash
export PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1
export PYTEST_PLUGINS=NAME,NAME2
pytest
```

```bash
pytest --disable-plugin-autoload -p NAME -p NAME2
```

```toml
[pytest]
addopts = ["--disable-plugin-autoload", "-p", "NAME", "-p", "NAME2"]
```

```ini
[pytest]
addopts =
    --disable-plugin-autoload
    -p NAME
    -p NAME2
```

### conftest.py plugin for directory manifest

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/example/customdirectory.rst

This conftest.py plugin defines a ManifestDirectory collector that reads a manifest.json file in each directory. It uses the pytest_collect_directory hook to replace the default directory collector. The plugin only supports a simple list of files, but can be extended with other keys like exclusions and globs.

```python
import json
from pathlib import Path

import pytest


class ManifestDirectory(pytest.Directory):
    def collect(self) -> Iterable[pytest.Collector]:
        # Read the manifest file.
        manifest_file = self.path / "manifest.json"
        manifest = json.loads(manifest_file.read_text())

        # Collect only the files listed in the manifest.
        for file in manifest["files"]:
            yield from self._collectfile(self.path / file)


def pytest_collect_directory(path: Path, parent: pytest.Collector) -> pytest.Collector | None:
    if (path / "manifest.json").is_file():
        return ManifestDirectory.from_parent(parent, path=path)
    return None
```

### Write failing test IDs to a file with pytest_runtest_makereport wrapper hook

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/example/simple.rst

A wrapper hook implementation that writes failing test node IDs to a 'failures' file, along with the test module and the command output showing the resulting file contents. The hook uses tryfirst=True and yields to obtain the report, then appends to the file only for failed 'call' phases, optionally including the tmp_path fixture value.

```python
import os.path

import pytest


@pytest.hookimpl(wrapper=True, tryfirst=True)
def pytest_runtest_makereport(item, call):
    # execute all other hooks to obtain the report object
    rep = yield

    # we only look at actual failing test calls, not setup/teardown
    if rep.when == "call" and rep.failed:
        mode = "a" if os.path.exists("failures") else "w"
        with open("failures", mode, encoding="utf-8") as f:
            # let's also access a fixture for the fun of it
            if "tmp_path" in item.fixturenames:
                extra = " ({})".format(item.funcargs["tmp_path"])
            else:
                extra = ""

            f.write(rep.nodeid + extra + "\n")

    return rep
```

```python
# content of test_module.py
def test_fail1(tmp_path):
    assert 0


def test_fail2():
    assert 0
```

```pytest
$ pytest test_module.py
=========================== test session starts ============================
platform linux -- Python 3.x.y, pytest-9.x.y, pluggy-1.x.y
rootdir: /home/sweet/project

================================= FAILURES =================================

tmp_path = PosixPath('PYTEST_TMPDIR/test_fail10')

>       assert 0
E       assert 0

test_module.py:2: AssertionError

### Request a fixture in a test function

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/fixtures.rst

Shows a basic pytest fixture: the test function requests the fixture by declaring it as an argument, and pytest runs the fixture and passes its return value into the test.

```python
import pytest


class Fruit:
    def __init__(self, name):
        self.name = name
        self.cubed = False

    def cube(self):
        self.cubed = True


class FruitSalad:
    def __init__(self, *fruit_bowl):
        self.fruit = fruit_bowl
        self._cube_fruit()

    def _cube_fruit(self):
        for fruit in self.fruit:
            fruit.cube()


# Arrange
@pytest.fixture
def fruit_bowl():
    return [Fruit("apple"), Fruit("banana")]


def test_fruit_salad(fruit_bowl):
    # Act
    fruit_salad = FruitSalad(*fruit_bowl)

    # Assert
    assert all(fruit.cubed for fruit in fruit_salad.fruit)
```

### Add --runslow option to control skipping of slow tests

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/example/simple.rst

conftest.py that adds a --runslow flag, registers a 'slow' marker, and skips tests marked slow unless the flag is given.

```python
# content of conftest.py

import pytest


def pytest_addoption(parser):
    parser.addoption(
        "--runslow", action="store_true", default=False, help="run slow tests"
    )


def pytest_configure(config):
    config.addinivalue_line("markers", "slow: mark test as slow to run")


def pytest_collection_modifyitems(config, items):
    if config.getoption("--runslow"):
        # --runslow given in cli: do not skip slow tests
        return
    skip_slow = pytest.mark.skip(reason="need --runslow option to run")
    for item in items:
        if item.get_closest_marker("slow"):
            item.add_marker(skip_slow)
```

### Pass data to fixtures using markers

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/fixtures.rst

The fixture uses request.node.get_closest_marker to read data from a marker applied to the test. If the marker is missing, data is set to None.

```python
import pytest


@pytest.fixture
def fixt(request):
    marker = request.node.get_closest_marker("fixt_data")
    if marker is None:
        # Handle missing marker in some way...
        data = None
    else:
        data = marker.args[0]

    # Do something with the data
    return data


@pytest.mark.fixt_data(42)
def test_fixt(fixt):
    assert fixt == 42

```

--------------------------------

### Test bad credentials with pytest.raises(BadCredentialsException)

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/fixtures.rst

Adds a test class for a bad-credentials scenario. The faux_user fixture deep-copies the user and changes the password, and the test expects LoginPage.login to raise BadCredentialsException. Assumes LoginPage raises that exception when it detects bad credentials.

```python
class TestLandingPageBadCredentials:
    @pytest.fixture(scope="class")
    @classmethod
    def faux_user(cls, user):
        _user = deepcopy(user)
        _user.password = "badpass"
        return _user

    def test_raises_bad_credentials_exception(self, login_page, faux_user):
        with pytest.raises(BadCredentialsException):
            login_page.login(faux_user)
```

--------------------------------

### Record warnings with recwarn fixture

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/capture-warnings.rst

Use the recwarn fixture to record warnings for the entire test function and inspect them. The fixture automatically resets the warnings filter at the end of the test.

```python
import warnings


def test_hello(recwarn):
    warnings.warn("hello", UserWarning)
    assert len(recwarn) == 1
    w = recwarn.pop(UserWarning)
    assert issubclass(w.category, UserWarning)
    assert str(w.message) == "hello"
    assert w.filename
    assert w.lineno
```

Define custom markers in the [pytest] section of a TOML configuration file. The optional description after the colon is used in help text and to suppress warnings.

```toml
[pytest]
markers = [
    "slow: marks tests as slow (deselect with '-m \"not slow\"')",
    "serial",
]
```

### Old ExceptionInfo str(e) usage

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/changelog.rst

Old code that inspected the exception message using str(e) on the ExceptionInfo object. This no longer works because ExceptionInfo's str representation now matches repr.

```python
with pytest.raises(SomeException) as e:
    ...
assert "some message" in str(e)
```

--------------------------------

### Command-line options for capturing methods

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/capture-stdout-stderr.rst

Shows how to disable or change capturing using pytest command-line options. -s disables all capturing, --capture=sys replaces sys.stdout/stderr with in-memory files, --capture=fd also redirects file descriptors 1 and 2, and --capture=tee-sys combines sys capturing with live output.

```bash
pytest -s                  # disable all capturing
pytest --capture=sys       # replace sys.stdout/stderr with in-mem files
pytest --capture=fd        # also point filedescriptors 1 and 2 to temp file
pytest --capture=tee-sys   # combines 'sys' and '-s', capturing sys.stdout/stderr
                           # and passing it along to the actual sys.stdout/stderr
```

    def func1():
        return 1
```

### Define autouse fixture to record test suite properties

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/output.rst

Defines a session-scoped autouse fixture that uses record_testsuite_property to add properties to the test suite XML. The fixture is called with name and value, where name must be a string and value is converted to a string and XML-escaped.

```python
@pytest.fixture(scope="session", autouse=True)
def log_global_env_facts(record_testsuite_property):
    record_testsuite_property("ARCH", "PPC")
    record_testsuite_property("STORAGE_TYPE", "CEPH")


class TestMe:
    def test_foo(self):
        assert True
```

### Override a fixture at the test module level

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/fixtures.rst

Shows a directory structure where a fixture named username is defined in tests/conftest.py and overridden in two test modules. Each module defines its own fixture with the same name and uses it in its test.

```text
tests/
    conftest.py
        # content of tests/conftest.py
        import pytest

        @pytest.fixture
        def username():
            return 'username'

    test_something.py
        # content of tests/test_something.py
        import pytest

        @pytest.fixture
        def username(username):
            return 'overridden-' + username

        def test_username(username):
            assert username == 'overridden-username'

    test_something_else.py
        # content of tests/test_something_else.py
        import pytest

        @pytest.fixture
        def username(username):
            return 'overridden-else-' + username

        def test_username(username):
            assert username == 'overridden-else-username'
```

### Parametrize all tests in a module with pytestmark

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/parametrize.rst

Uses the pytestmark global variable to parametrize all tests in a module. The class TestClass contains two test methods, each run with the two parameter sets.

```python
import pytest

pytestmark = pytest.mark.parametrize("n,expected", [(1, 2), (3, 4)])


class TestClass:
    def test_simple_case(self, n, expected):
        assert n + 1 == expected

    def test_weird_simple_case(self, n, expected):
        assert (n * 1) + 1 == expected
```

### Set doctest report format with --doctest-report

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/doctest.rst

Change the diff output format on doctest failure using the standard doctest module's format options: none, udiff, cdiff, ndiff, or only_first_failure.

```bash
pytest --doctest-modules --doctest-report none
pytest --doctest-modules --doctest-report udiff
pytest --doctest-modules --doctest-report cdiff
pytest --doctest-modules --doctest-report ndiff
pytest --doctest-modules --doctest-report only_first_failure
```

### Implement platform-specific test skipping

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/example/markers.rst

Use a conftest.py hook to skip tests based on platform markers, ensuring tests only run on their designated operating systems.

```python
# content of conftest.py
#
import sys

import pytest

ALL = set("darwin linux win32".split())


def pytest_runtest_setup(item):
    supported_platforms = ALL.intersection(mark.name for mark in item.iter_markers())
    plat = sys.platform
    if supported_platforms and plat not in supported_platforms:
        pytest.skip(f"cannot run on platform {plat}")
```

```python
# content of test_plat.py

import pytest


@pytest.mark.darwin
def test_if_apple_is_evil():
    pass


@pytest.mark.linux
def test_if_linux_works():
    pass


@pytest.mark.win32
def test_if_win32_crashes():
    pass


def test_runs_everywhere():
    pass
```

```pytest
$ pytest -rs # this option reports skip reasons
=========================== test session starts ============================
platform linux -- Python 3.x.y, pytest-9.x.y, pluggy-1.x.y
rootdir: /home/sweet/project
collected 4 items

test_plat.py s.s.                                                    [100%]

========================= short test summary info ==========================
SKIPPED [2] conftest.py:13: cannot run on platform linux
======================= 2 passed, 2 skipped in 0.12s =======================
```

### Apply marks to parametrized fixture values with pytest.param

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/fixtures.rst

Uses `pytest.param` to apply marks (here `pytest.mark.skip`) to a specific value in a parametrized fixture. Running `pytest test_fixture_marks.py -v` skips the invocation with value `2`, resulting in 2 passed and 1 skipped.

```python
# content of test_fixture_marks.py
import pytest


@pytest.fixture(params=[0, 1, pytest.param(2, marks=pytest.mark.skip)])
def data_set(request):
    return request.param


def test_data(data_set):
    pass
```

```

### Introspect requesting test context with request.module

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/fixtures.rst

The fixture reads an optional server URL from the requesting test module via request.module. The test module sets the smtpserver attribute, which the fixture picks up.

```python
# content of conftest.py
import smtplib

import pytest


@pytest.fixture(scope="module")
def smtp_connection(request):
    server = getattr(request.module, "smtpserver", "smtp.gmail.com")
    smtp_connection = smtplib.SMTP(server, 587, timeout=5)
    yield smtp_connection
    print(f"finalizing {smtp_connection} ({server})")
    smtp_connection.close()

```

```python
# content of test_anothersmtp.py

smtpserver = "mail.python.org"  # will be read by smtp fixture


def test_showhelo(smtp_connection):
    assert 0, smtp_connection.helo()

```

### pytest --markers output

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/example/markers.rst

The output of the '--markers' option, which lists all available markers including the custom 'env' marker and built-in ones.

```pytest
$ pytest --markers
@pytest.mark.env(name): mark test to run only on named environment

@pytest.mark.filterwarnings(warning): add a warning filter to the given test. see https://docs.pytest.org/en/stable/how-to/capture-warnings.html#pytest-mark-filterwarnings

@pytest.mark.skip(reason=None): skip the given test function with an optional reason. Example: skip(reason="no way of currently testing this") skips the test.

@pytest.mark.skipif(condition, ..., *, reason=...): skip the given test function if any of the conditions evaluate to True. Example: skipif(sys.platform == 'win32') skips the test if we are on the win32 platform. See https://docs.pytest.org/en/stable/reference/reference.html#pytest-mark-skipif

@pytest.mark.xfail(condition, ..., *, reason=..., run=True, raises=None, strict=strict_xfail): mark the test function as an expected failure if any of the conditions evaluate to True. Optionally specify a reason for better reporting and run=False if you don't even want to execute the test function. If only specific exception(s) are expected, you can list them in raises, and if the test fails in other ways, it will be reported as a true failure. See https://docs.pytest.org/en/stable/reference/reference.html#pytest-mark-xfail

@pytest.mark.parametrize(argnames, argvalues): call a test function multiple times passing in different arguments in turn. argvalues generally needs to be a list of values if argnames specifies only one name or a list of tuples of values if argnames specifies multiple names. Example: @parametrize('arg1', [1,2]) would lead to two calls of the decorated test function, one with arg1=1 and another with arg1=2.see https://docs.pytest.org/en/stable/how-to/parametrize.html for more info and examples.

@pytest.mark.usefixtures(fixturename1, fixturename2, ...): mark tests as needing all of the specified fixtures. see https://docs.pytest.org/en/stable/explanation/fixtures.html#usefixtures

@pytest.mark.tryfirst: mark a hook implementation function such that the plugin machinery will try to call it first/as early as possible. DEPRECATED, use @pytest.hookimpl(tryfirst=True) instead.

@pytest.mark.trylast: mark a hook implementation function such that the plugin machinery will try to call it last/as late as possible. DEPRECATED, use @pytest.hookimpl(trylast=True) instead.
```

### Standalone layout and pytest invocation for prepend/append import modes

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/explanation/pythonpath.rst

Shows a standalone layout where test modules and conftest.py files are not inside packages. When running 'pytest root/', pytest adds 'root/foo/bar/tests' to sys.path to import test_foo.py as 'test_foo' and adds 'root/foo' to import conftest.py as 'conftest'. This layout cannot have test modules with the same name.

```text
root/
|- foo/
   |- conftest.py
   |- bar/
      |- tests/
         |- test_foo.py

```

```bash
pytest root/
```

### required_plugins configuration in TOML

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/reference/reference.rst

Sets a space-separated list of required plugins with optional version specifiers. If any plugin is missing, pytest emits an error. Whitespace between version specifiers is not allowed.

```toml
[pytest]
required_plugins = ["pytest-django>=3.0.0,<4.0.0", "pytest-html", "pytest-xdist>=1.0.0"]
```

### pytest.warns

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/capture-warnings.rst

Context manager to assert that code raises a particular warning and optionally capture the warnings.

```APIDOC
## pytest.warns

### Description
Checks that code raises a particular warning. Works similarly to `pytest.raises` but captures warnings. The test will fail if the expected warning is not raised.

### Usage
Use as a context manager around the code that should emit the warning.

### Parameters
- **expected_warning** (type) - Optional - The expected warning class. Defaults to `Warning` if not provided.
- **match** (str) - Optional - A regex pattern that the warning message must match. Use `re.escape` for literal strings with regex metacharacters.

### Returns
A list of `warnings.WarningMessage` objects for the raised warnings.

### Examples
```python
import warnings
import pytest

def test_warning():
    with pytest.warns(UserWarning):
        warnings.warn("my warning", UserWarning)
```

```python
with pytest.warns(UserWarning, match="must be 0 or None"):
    warnings.warn("value must be 0 or None", UserWarning)
```

```python
with pytest.warns(RuntimeWarning) as record:
    warnings.warn("another warning", RuntimeWarning)
assert len(record) == 1
assert record[0].message.args[0] == "another warning"
```

```python
with pytest.warns() as record:
    warnings.warn("user", UserWarning)
    warnings.warn("runtime", RuntimeWarning)
assert len(record) == 2
assert str(record[0].message) == "user"
assert str(record[1].message) == "runtime"
```

### Behavior
- Fails if the expected warning is not raised.
- If `match` is provided, the warning message must match the regex pattern.
```

--------------------------------

### Automatic grouping of tests by fixture instances

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/fixtures.rst

Shows how pytest minimizes active fixtures by running all tests using a parametrized fixture instance before finalizing it. The module-scoped modarg fixture is set up once per module parameter, while the function-scoped otherarg is set up and torn down for each test. Run with `pytest -v -s test_module.py` to see the setup/teardown order.

```python
# content of test_module.py
import pytest


@pytest.fixture(scope="module", params=["mod1", "mod2"])
def modarg(request):
    param = request.param
    print("  SETUP modarg", param)
    yield param
    print("  TEARDOWN modarg", param)


@pytest.fixture(scope="function", params=[1, 2])
def otherarg(request):
    param = request.param
    print("  SETUP otherarg", param)
    yield param
    print("  TEARDOWN otherarg", param)


def test_0(otherarg):
    print("  RUN test0 with otherarg", otherarg)


def test_1(modarg):
    print("  RUN test1 with modarg", modarg)


def test_2(otherarg, modarg):
    print(f"  RUN test2 with otherarg {otherarg} and modarg {modarg}")
```

```pytest
$ pytest -v -s test_module.py
=========================== test session starts ============================
platform linux -- Python 3.x.y, pytest-9.x.y, pluggy-1.x.y -- $PYTHON_PREFIX/bin/python
cachedir: .pytest_cache
rootdir: /home/sweet/project
collecting ... collected 8 items

test_module.py::test_0[1]   SETUP otherarg 1
  RUN test0 with otherarg 1
PASSED  TEARDOWN otherarg 1

test_module.py::test_0[2]   SETUP otherarg 2
  RUN test0 with otherarg 2
PASSED  TEARDOWN otherarg 2

test_module.py::test_1[mod1]   SETUP modarg mod1
  RUN test1 with modarg mod1
PASSED
test_module.py::test_2[mod1-1]   SETUP otherarg 1
  RUN test2 with otherarg 1 and modarg mod1
PASSED  TEARDOWN otherarg 1

test_module.py::test_2[mod1-2]   SETUP otherarg 2
  RUN test2 with otherarg 2 and modarg mod1
PASSED  TEARDOWN otherarg 2

test_module.py::test_1[mod2]   TEARDOWN modarg mod1
  SETUP modarg mod2
  RUN test1 with modarg mod2
PASSED
test_module.py::test_2[mod2-1]   SETUP otherarg 1
  RUN test2 with otherarg 1 and modarg mod2
PASSED  TEARDOWN otherarg 1

test_module.py::test_2[mod2-2]   SETUP otherarg 2
  RUN test2 with otherarg 2 and modarg mod2
PASSED  TEARDOWN otherarg 2
  TEARDOWN modarg mod2


============================ 8 passed in 0.12s =============================
```

--------------------------------

### Run tests in class and see failure

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/getting-started.rst

Run the test_class.py module. The output shows the first test passed and the second failed, with intermediate values in the assertion to help understand the failure.

```pytest
$ pytest -q test_class.py

### xfail with raises parameter

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/skipping.rst

Specify a single exception or a tuple of exceptions in the raises argument to be more specific about why the test is failing. If the test fails with an exception not mentioned, it will be reported as a regular failure.

```python
@pytest.mark.xfail(raises=RuntimeError)
def test_function(): ...
```

--------------------------------

### conftest.py with basemod and optmod fixtures

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/example/parametrize.rst

Defines session-scoped fixtures that use pytest.importorskip to conditionally import modules. basemod imports 'base', while optmod is parametrized over 'opt1' and 'opt2' and skips if the module is not available.

```python
    # content of conftest.py

    import pytest


    @pytest.fixture(scope="session")
    def basemod(request):
        return pytest.importorskip("base")


    @pytest.fixture(scope="session", params=["opt1", "opt2"])
    def optmod(request):
        return pytest.importorskip(request.param)
```

--------------------------------

### Replace string argument in raises/warns

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/deprecations.rst

The string form of raises/warns is deprecated; use the context manager form and invoke exec directly when necessary.

```python
pytest.raises(ZeroDivisionError, "1 / 0")
pytest.raises(SyntaxError, "a $ b")

pytest.warns(DeprecationWarning, "my_function()")
pytest.warns(SyntaxWarning, "assert(1, 2)")
```

```python
with pytest.raises(ZeroDivisionError):
    1 / 0
with pytest.raises(SyntaxError):
    exec("a $ b")  # exec is required for invalid syntax

with pytest.warns(DeprecationWarning):
    my_function()
with pytest.warns(SyntaxWarning):
    exec("assert(1, 2)")  # exec is used to avoid a top-level warning
```
