### Custom Item constructor with **kwargs

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/deprecations.rst

Recommended constructor for custom pytest.Item subclass that accepts **kwargs to avoid hard-coding arguments pytest can pass to the superclass. No deprecation warning is emitted for cases without conflicts.

```python
class CustomItem(pytest.Item):
    def __init__(self, *, additional_arg, **kwargs):
        super().__init__(**kwargs)
        self.additional_arg = additional_arg
```

--------------------------------

### Removing dictionary keys with monkeypatch.delitem - test_app.py

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/monkeypatch.rst

Uses monkeypatch.delitem to remove the 'user' key from app.DEFAULT_CONFIG for a test, with raising=False to avoid an error if the key is missing. Verifies that OSError is raised when the key is absent.

```python
# contents of test_app.py
import pytest

# app.py with the connection string function
import app


def test_missing_user(monkeypatch):
    # patch the DEFAULT_CONFIG to be missing the 'user' key
    monkeypatch.delitem(app.DEFAULT_CONFIG, "user", raising=False)
```

--------------------------------

### Using pytest.raises with match parameter

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/assert.rst

Shows how to use the match parameter in pytest.raises to test that a regular expression matches the string representation of an exception. The match parameter is matched with re.search, so a partial match like '123' would also work.

```python
import pytest


def myfunc():
    raise ValueError("Exception 123 raised")


def test_match():
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match=r".* 123 .*"):
        myfunc()
```

--------------------------------

### monkeypatch

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/builtin.rst

Fixture that provides methods for monkey-patching.

```APIDOC
## Fixture: monkeypatch

### Description
A convenient fixture for monkey-patching. Provides methods to modify objects, dictionaries, or os.environ.

### Methods
- monkeypatch.setattr(obj, name, value, raising=True)
- monkeypatch.delattr(obj, name, raising=True)
- monkeypatch.setitem(mapping, name, value)
- monkeypatch.delitem(obj, name, raising=True)
- monkeypatch.setenv(name, value, prepend=None)
- monkeypatch.delenv(name, raising=True)
- monkeypatch.syspath_prepend(path)
- monkeypatch.chdir(path)
- monkeypatch.context()

### Behavior
All modifications are undone after the requesting test function or fixture has finished. The raising parameter determines if a KeyError or AttributeError will be raised if the set/deletion operation does not have the specified target.

### Example
```python
def test_foo(monkeypatch):
    monkeypatch.setattr(obj, "attr", value)
```
```

### Test failing assertion with output matching

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/CONTRIBUTING.rst

Use this test to verify that a failing assertion produces the expected output. It creates a test with assert False and checks that the output contains the assertion and a failure count using glob-like patterns.

```python
def test_true_assertion(pytester):
    pytester.makepyfile(
        """
        def test_foo():
            assert False
    """
    )
    result = pytester.runpytest()
    result.stdout.fnmatch_lines(["*assert False*", "*1 failed*"])
```

### Assert on captured log records and text

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/logging.rst

Accesses all logs sent to the logger during the test run via caplog.records and caplog.text to assert on the contents of messages.

def test_baz(caplog):
    func_under_test()
    for record in caplog.records:
        assert record.levelname != "CRITICAL"
    assert "wally" not in caplog.text

### Parametrize a test function with @pytest.mark.parametrize

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/parametrize.rst

Shows a basic @pytest.mark.parametrize usage with a test function and the resulting pytest output. The test runs three times; the third case fails because eval('6*9') returns 54, not 42. Parameter values are passed as-is without copying, so mutating a list or dict parameter affects subsequent test calls.

```python
# content of test_expectation.py
import pytest


@pytest.mark.parametrize("test_input,expected", [("3+5", 8), ("2+4", 6), ("6*9", 42)])
def test_eval(test_input, expected):
    assert eval(test_input) == expected
```

```pytest
$ pytest
=========================== test session starts ============================
platform linux -- Python 3.x.y, pytest-9.x.y, pluggy-1.x.y
rootdir: /home/sweet/project
collected 3 items

test_expectation.py ..F                                              [100%]

================================= FAILURES =================================
____________________________ test_eval[6*9-42] _____________________________

test_input = '6*9', expected = 42

    @pytest.mark.parametrize("test_input,expected", [("3+5", 8), ("2+4", 6), ("6*9", 42)])
    def test_eval(test_input, expected):
>       assert eval(test_input) == expected
E       AssertionError: assert 54 == 42
E        +  where 54 = eval('6*9')

test_expectation.py:6: AssertionError
========================= short test summary info ==========================
FAILED test_expectation.py::test_eval[6*9-42] - AssertionError: assert 54...
======================= 1 failed, 2 passed in 0.12s ========================
```

### pytestconfig

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/builtin.rst

Session-scoped fixture that provides access to the pytest configuration object.

```APIDOC
## Fixture: pytestconfig

### Description
Session-scoped fixture that returns the session's pytest.Config object.

### Usage
Declare as a fixture parameter in a test function or fixture to access the configuration object.

### Example
```python
def test_foo(pytestconfig):
    if pytestconfig.get_verbosity() > 0:
        ...
```
```

--------------------------------

### Deprecated: calling fixture directly

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/deprecations.rst

Deprecated pattern: calling a fixture function directly inside another fixture. This breaks the fixture resolution model and is a source of confusion for new users.

```python
@pytest.fixture
    def cell():
        return ...


    @pytest.fixture
    def full_cell():
        cell = cell()
        cell.make_full()
        return cell
```

--------------------------------

### xfail with strict=True parameter

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/skipping.rst

Set the strict keyword-only parameter to True to make XPASS results from this test fail the test suite. By default, XFAIL and XPASS do not fail the suite.

```python
@pytest.mark.xfail(strict=True)
def test_function(): ...
```

### Enable individual strictness options in pyproject.toml

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/explanation/goodpractices.rst

Enable specific strictness options individually in a TOML configuration file to avoid automatically picking up new options added in future pytest versions.

```toml
[pytest]
strict_config = true
strict_markers = true
strict_parametrization_ids = true
strict_xfail = true
```

## Fixture: doctest_namespace

Provides a dictionary injected into the namespace of doctests, allowing tests to access additional objects.

```

--------------------------------

### Parametrizing conditional raising with pytest.raises and nullcontext

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/example/parametrize.rst

Uses pytest.raises and contextlib.nullcontext as expectation values in a parametrized test. The first three cases run without exceptions, while the fourth expects a ZeroDivisionError.

```python
from contextlib import nullcontext

import pytest


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    "example_input,expectation",
    [
        (3, nullcontext(2)),
        (2, nullcontext(3)),
        (1, nullcontext(6)),
        (0, pytest.raises(ZeroDivisionError)),
    ],
)
def test_division(example_input, expectation):
    """Test how much I know division."""
    with expectation as e:
        assert (6 / example_input) == e
```

### Require plugins with pytest_plugins variable

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/plugins.rst

Require plugins in a test module or conftest file. When the module or conftest is loaded, the specified plugins are loaded as well. Note that using this in non-root conftest.py files is deprecated, and the name pytest_plugins is reserved.

```python
pytest_plugins = ("myapp.testsupport.myplugin",)
```

### Use pytest.param for parametrize marks

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/deprecations.rst

Use pytest.param to mark a specific parametrize case with xfail, replacing the deprecated marks argument in parametrize.

```python
@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    "a, b",
    [
        (3, 9),
        pytest.param(6, 36, marks=pytest.mark.xfail(reason="flaky")),
        (10, 100),
        (20, 200),
        (40, 400),
        (50, 500),
    ],
)
def test_foo(a, b): ...
```

with pytest.raises(ExceptionGroup[Exception]) as exc_info:
    some_function()

### Type annotation with pytest.Subtests

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/subtests.rst

--------------------------------

### Applying mark to fixture function (deprecated)

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/deprecations.rst

Applying a mark to a fixture function never had any effect. pytest now issues a warning and will raise an error in future versions.

```python
@pytest.mark.usefixtures("clean_database")
@pytest.fixture
def user() -> User: ...
```

--------------------------------

### New node id without ::() notation

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/changelog.rst

New node id format after removal of the ::() notation. The extra ::() has been removed, so test ids now use this format.

```text
test_foo.py::Test::test_bar
```

--------------------------------

### Temporarily change log level with caplog.at_level

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/logging.rst

Uses a context manager to temporarily change the log level inside a with block. By default the root logger is affected.

```python
def test_bar(caplog):
    with caplog.at_level(logging.INFO):
        pass
```

### Deprecated record_xml_property fixture

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/deprecations.rst

Deprecated record_xml_property fixture usage.

```python
def test_foo(record_xml_property): ...
```

### Register custom markers in pytest.toml

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/example/markers.rst

Registers custom markers 'webtest' and 'slow' in a pytest.toml file. Each marker is defined on its own line.

```toml
# content of pytest.toml
[pytest]
markers = ["webtest: mark a test as a webtest.", "slow: mark test as slow."]
```

--------------------------------

### Disable plugin autoload and load specific plugins

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/plugins.rst

These commands disable plugin auto-loading and explicitly load plugins for a single pytest invocation. The first uses -p to load a plugin by name, while the second uses the PYTEST_PLUGINS environment variable to load plugin modules during startup. Avoid specifying the same plugin via multiple mechanisms to prevent registration errors.

```bash
# Disable auto-loading and load only specific plugins for this invocation
PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 pytest -p xdist
```

```bash
# Disable auto-loading and load plugin modules during startup
PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 PYTEST_PLUGINS=mymodule.plugin,xdist pytest
```

### fixture: pytester

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/reference/reference.rst

The pytester fixture provides a Pytester instance for running and testing pytest itself. It offers an isolated directory and facilities to write tests, configuration files, and match output.

```APIDOC
## Fixture: pytester

### Description
Provides a Pytester instance for running and testing pytest itself. It offers an isolated directory and facilities to write tests, configuration files, and match output.

### Usage
Declare as a fixture parameter in a test function.

### Version Added
6.2

### Setup
Include in your topmost conftest.py:

```python
pytest_plugins = "pytester"
```
```

### Custom fixture parameter IDs with ids list or function

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/fixtures.rst

Shows how the `ids` parameter of `@pytest.fixture` can be a list of strings or a function that returns a string (or `None` to use pytest's auto-generated ID). The output of `pytest --collect-only` demonstrates the resulting test IDs: `test_a[spam]`, `test_a[ham]`, `test_b[eggs]`, and `test_b[1]`.

```python
# content of test_ids.py
import pytest


@pytest.fixture(params=[0, 1], ids=["spam", "ham"])
def a(request):
    return request.param


def test_a(a):
    pass


def idfn(fixture_value):
    if fixture_value == 0:
        return "eggs"
    else:
        return None


@pytest.fixture(params=[0, 1], ids=idfn)
def b(request):
    return request.param


def test_b(b):
    pass
```

```pytest
$ pytest --collect-only
=========================== test session starts ============================
platform linux -- Python 3.x.y, pytest-9.x.y, pluggy-1.x.y
rootdir: /home/sweet/project
collected 12 items

<Dir fixtures.rst-236>
  <Module test_anothersmtp.py>
    <Function test_showhelo[smtp.gmail.com]>
    <Function test_showhelo[mail.python.org]>
  <Module test_emaillib.py>
    <Function test_email_received>
  <Module test_finalizers.py>
    <Function test_bar>
  <Module test_ids.py>
    <Function test_a[spam]>
    <Function test_a[ham]>
    <Function test_b[eggs]>
    <Function test_b[1]>
  <Module test_module.py>
    <Function test_ehlo[smtp.gmail.com]>
    <Function test_noop[smtp.gmail.com]>
    <Function test_ehlo[mail.python.org]>
    <Function test_noop[mail.python.org]>

======================= 12 tests collected in 0.12s ========================
```

### Ensure no warnings with warnings.simplefilter("error")

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/capture-warnings.rst

Use this to ensure no warnings are emitted. The simplefilter("error") turns warnings into exceptions, causing the test to fail if any warning occurs.

```python
def test_warning():
    with warnings.catch_warnings():
        warnings.simplefilter("error")
        ...
```

### Check for from_parent with hasattr

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/deprecations.rst

Use hasattr to check for from_parent before calling it, since from_parent should only be called with keyword arguments.

```python
def pytest_pycollect_makeitem(collector, name, obj):
    if hasattr(MyItem, "from_parent"):
        item = MyItem.from_parent(collector, name="foo")
        item.obj = 42
        return item
    else:
        return MyItem(name="foo", parent=collector, obj=42)
```

### required_plugins configuration in INI

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/reference/reference.rst

Sets a space-separated list of required plugins with optional version specifiers. If any plugin is missing, pytest emits an error. Whitespace between version specifiers is not allowed.

```ini
[pytest]
required_plugins = pytest-django>=3.0.0,<4.0.0 pytest-html pytest-xdist>=1.0.0
```

--------------------------------

### Disable a plugin in pytest configuration (TOML and INI)

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/plugins.rst

Unconditionally disable a plugin for a project by adding the -p no:NAME option to the configuration file. The TOML and INI formats are shown as alternatives.

```toml
[pytest]
addopts = ["-p", "no:NAME"]
```

```ini
[pytest]
addopts = -p no:NAME
```

--------------------------------

### xfail with condition parameter

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/skipping.rst

Pass a condition as the first parameter to @pytest.mark.xfail to only expect failure under that condition. A reason must also be provided.

```python
@pytest.mark.xfail(sys.platform == "win32", reason="bug in a 3rd party library")
def test_function(): ...
```

### Return multiple header lines conditionally with pytest_report_header

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/example/simple.rst

Return a list of strings from pytest_report_header to add multiple lines. Use config.get_verbosity() to conditionally include extra info when verbose mode is enabled.

```python
# content of conftest.py


def pytest_report_header(config):
    if config.get_verbosity() > 0:
        return ["info1: did you know that ...", "did you?"]
```

--------------------------------

### Test environment variables with setenv and delenv - test_code.py

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/monkeypatch.rst

Tests the two paths of get_os_user_lower(): setting the USER environment variable with monkeypatch.setenv and deleting it with monkeypatch.delenv. Uses pytest.raises to assert that OSError is raised when the variable is missing.

```python
# contents of our test file e.g. test_code.py
import pytest


def test_upper_to_lower(monkeypatch):
    """Set the USER env var to assert the behavior."""
    monkeypatch.setenv("USER", "TestingUser")
    assert get_os_user_lower() == "testinguser"


def test_raise_exception(monkeypatch):
    """Remove the USER env var and assert OSError is raised."""
    monkeypatch.delenv("USER", raising=False)

    with pytest.raises(OSError):
        _ = get_os_user_lower()
```

--------------------------------

### caplog.at_level(level, logger=None)

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/logging.rst

Context manager to temporarily change the log level for captured log messages within a `with` block. By default, it affects the root logger, but a specific logger can be targeted. The level is restored when exiting the context.

```APIDOC
## caplog.at_level(level, logger=None)

### Description
Context manager to temporarily change the log level inside a `with` block. By default, it affects the root logger, but a specific logger can be targeted using the `logger` parameter. The log level is restored when exiting the context.

### Parameters
- **level** (int) - Required - The logging level to set (e.g., `logging.INFO`).
- **logger** (str) - Optional - The name of the logger to set the level on. If not provided, the root logger is used.

### Example
```python
def test_bar(caplog):
    with caplog.at_level(logging.INFO):
        pass
```

### Notes
- The log level is restored automatically when exiting the `with` block.
- The `logger` parameter allows setting the level on any logger, e.g., `caplog.at_level(logging.CRITICAL, logger="root.baz")`.
```

### Set empty_parameter_set_mark to xfail in pyproject.toml

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/reference/reference.rst

Configure the action for empty parameter sets via TOML. The default is "skip"; "xfail" marks tests with an empty parameter set as xfail(run=False). The default is planned to change to "xfail" in future releases.

### Use pytest.param to apply marks or set test ID

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/example/parametrize.rst

Parametrizes a test with pytest.param to apply custom marks (basic) and built-in marks (xfail) to individual test cases, and to set explicit test IDs. Running `pytest -v -m basic` selects only the three marked tests, with the xfail test expected to fail.

```python
# content of test_pytest_param_example.py
import pytest


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    "test_input,expected",
    [
        ("3+5", 8),
        pytest.param("1+7", 8, marks=pytest.mark.basic),
        pytest.param("2+4", 6, marks=pytest.mark.basic, id="basic_2+4"),
        pytest.param(
            "6*9", 42, marks=[pytest.mark.basic, pytest.mark.xfail], id="basic_6*9"
        ),
    ],
)
def test_eval(test_input, expected):
    assert eval(test_input) == expected
```

```pytest
$ pytest -v -m basic
=========================== test session starts ============================
platform linux -- Python 3.x.y, pytest-9.x.y, pluggy-1.x.y -- $PYTHON_PREFIX/bin/python
cachedir: .pytest_cache
rootdir: /home/sweet/project
collecting ... collected 24 items / 21 deselected / 3 selected

test_pytest_param_example.py::test_eval[1+7-8] PASSED                [ 33%]
test_pytest_param_example.py::test_eval[basic_2+4] PASSED            [ 66%]
test_pytest_param_example.py::test_eval[basic_6*9] XFAIL             [100%]

=============== 2 passed, 21 deselected, 1 xfailed in 0.12s ================
```

### Assert exception with pytest.raises in test_sysexit.py

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/getting-started.rst

Use the pytest.raises helper to assert that a specific exception is raised. This test expects SystemExit to be raised by the f() function.

```python
# content of test_sysexit.py
import pytest


def f():
    raise SystemExit(1)


def test_mytest():
    with pytest.raises(SystemExit):
        f()
```

--------------------------------

### After: class-scoped fixture with @classmethod (recommended)

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/deprecations.rst

Deprecated in 9.1. Use @classmethod to set attributes on the class itself, making them accessible to all test methods. This is the recommended approach for class-scoped fixtures.

```python
class TestExample:
    @pytest.fixture(scope="class")
    @classmethod
    def setup_data(cls):
        cls.data = [1, 2, 3]

    def test_something(self, setup_data):
        assert self.data == [1, 2, 3]  # Works correctly
```

```

### pytest.define_combined_fixture proposal

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/proposals/parametrize_with_fixtures.rst

Proposes a new function pytest.define_combined_fixture to dynamically define a fixture from existing ones. The new fixture 'context' inherits the scope from the used fixtures and yields values from both default_context and extra_context.

```python
pytest.define_combined_fixture(
    name="context", fixtures=["default_context", "extra_context"]
)

```

### Register assertion rewrite in pytest_foo/__init__.py

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/writing_plugins.rst

Call pytest.register_assert_rewrite in the package __init__.py to enable assertion rewriting for a helper module before it is imported. This is needed because only conftest.py files and modules listed in the pytest11 entry point are rewritten by default.

```python
import pytest

pytest.register_assert_rewrite("pytest_foo.helper")
```

--------------------------------

### Stopgap: inject namespace in pytest_configure

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/deprecations.rst

Stopgap measure for plugin authors to inject names into pytest's namespace during pytest_configure, as an alternative to the deprecated pytest_namespace hook.

```python
import pytest


    def pytest_configure():
        pytest.my_symbol = MySymbol()
```

--------------------------------

### pytest traceback printing options

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/output.rst

Command-line options to control traceback printing: --showlocals/-l to show local variables, --capture to set capture mode, and --tb to set traceback format. --full-trace prints very long traces and ensures a stack trace on KeyboardInterrupt.

```bash
pytest --showlocals     # show local variables in tracebacks
pytest -l               # show local variables (shortcut)
pytest --no-showlocals  # hide local variables (if addopts enables them)

pytest --capture=fd  # default, capture at the file descriptor level
pytest --capture=sys # capture at the sys level
pytest --capture=no  # don't capture
pytest -s            # don't capture (shortcut)
pytest --capture=tee-sys # capture to logs but also output to sys level streams

pytest --tb=auto    # (default) 'long' tracebacks for the first and last
                     # entry, but 'short' style for the other entries
pytest --tb=long    # exhaustive, informative traceback formatting
pytest --tb=short   # shorter traceback format
pytest --tb=line    # only one line per failure
pytest --tb=native  # Python standard library formatting
pytest --tb=no      # no traceback at all
```

### Requesting a unique temporary directory with tmp_path

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/getting-started.rst

Shows how to use the built-in tmp_path fixture by listing it in the test function signature. pytest creates a unique-per-test temporary directory before the test runs.

```python
# content of test_tmp_path.py
def test_needsfiles(tmp_path):
    print(tmp_path)
    assert 0
```

--------------------------------

### Replace msg= with reason= in pytest.fail, pytest.skip, pytest.exit

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/deprecations.rst

Shows the deprecated msg keyword argument and the recommended reason argument for pytest.fail, pytest.skip, and pytest.exit. The msg parameter is deprecated since pytest 7.0 and removed in 8.0; use reason instead for consistency with @pytest.mark.skip and @pytest.mark.xfail.

```python
def test_fail_example():
    # old
    pytest.fail(msg="foo")
    # new
    pytest.fail(reason="bar")


def test_skip_example():
    # old
    pytest.skip(msg="foo")
    # new
    pytest.skip(reason="bar")


def test_exit_example():
    # old
    pytest.exit(msg="foo")
    # new
    pytest.exit(reason="bar")
```

### Override configuration options with -o

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/reference/reference.rst

Use the -o/--override-ini option to override configuration values using option=value syntax. Can be specified multiple times.

```bash
pytest -o strict_xfail=true -o cache_dir=cache
```

--------------------------------

### Requesting a fixture multiple times (cached return values)

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/fixtures.rst

Shows that fixtures requested multiple times during a test are cached, so the same object is shared. The 'append_first' fixture modifies 'order', and the test sees the effect because 'order' is cached.

```python
# contents of test_append.py
import pytest


# Arrange
@pytest.fixture
def first_entry():
    return "a"


# Arrange
@pytest.fixture
def order():
    return []


# Act
@pytest.fixture
def append_first(order, first_entry):
    return order.append(first_entry)



def test_string_only(append_first, order, first_entry):
    # Assert
    assert order == [first_entry]
```

--------------------------------

### Dynamic fixture scope with determine_scope

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/fixtures.rst

Defines a fixture with a dynamic scope based on a command-line option. The callable `determine_scope` is executed once during fixture definition and returns either "session" or "function" depending on whether `--keep-containers` is provided.

```python
def determine_scope(fixture_name, config):
    if config.getoption("--keep-containers", None):
        return "session"
    return "function"


@pytest.fixture(scope=determine_scope)
def docker_container():
    yield spawn_container()
```

    your_test_method()

### Python test file showing automatic warning capture

```python

def api_v1():
    warnings.warn(UserWarning("api v1, should use functions from v2"))
    return 1


def test_one():
    assert api_v1() == 1
```

### Checking exact exception types

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/assert.rst

Since pytest.raises matches subclasses, verify the exact exception type by checking the excinfo.type attribute.

```python
def test_foo_not_implemented():
    def foo():
        raise NotImplementedError

    with pytest.raises(RuntimeError) as excinfo:
        foo()
    assert excinfo.type is RuntimeError
```

### Define custom marker with with_args

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/example/markers.rst

Use with_args to pass a callable as a positional argument to a custom marker without triggering decorator behavior.

```python
# content of test_custom_marker.py
import pytest


def hello_world(*args, **kwargs):
    return "Hello World"


@pytest.mark.my_marker.with_args(hello_world)
def test_with_args():
    pass
```

```pytest
$ pytest -q -s
Mark(name='my_marker', args=(<function hello_world at 0xdeadbeef0001>,), kwargs={})
.
1 passed in 0.12s
```

Previous idiom for asserting that captured text does not match a pattern, using re.match. The new LineMatcher.no_fnmatch_line and no_re_match_line functions produce better output on failure.

```python
result = testdir.runpytest()
assert re.match(pat, result.stdout.str()) is None
```

### Record warnings with pytest.warns

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/capture-warnings.rst

Record warnings without asserting a specific type by passing no arguments to pytest.warns(), which defaults to a generic Warning.

```python
with pytest.warns() as record:
    warnings.warn("user", UserWarning)
    warnings.warn("runtime", RuntimeWarning)

assert len(record) == 2
assert str(record[0].message) == "user"
assert str(record[1].message) == "runtime"
```

--------------------------------

### Share a mock via a pytest fixture

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/monkeypatch.rst

Refactors the previous mock into a pytest fixture so it can be shared across tests. The fixture uses monkeypatch to replace requests.get, and the test function takes the fixture as an argument instead of using monkeypatch directly.

```python
# contents of test_app.py, a simple test for our API retrieval
import pytest
import requests

# app.py that includes the get_json() function
import app


# custom class to be the mock return value of requests.get()
class MockResponse:
    @staticmethod
    def json():
        return {"mock_key": "mock_response"}


# monkeypatched requests.get moved to a fixture
@pytest.fixture
def mock_response(monkeypatch):
    """Requests.get() mocked to return {'mock_key':'mock_response'}."""

    def mock_get(*args, **kwargs):
        return MockResponse()

    monkeypatch.setattr(requests, "get", mock_get)


# notice our test uses the custom fixture instead of monkeypatch directly
def test_get_json(mock_response):
    result = app.get_json("https://fakeurl")
    assert result["mock_key"] == "mock_response"
```

### pytest.mark.xfail with RaisesGroup

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/assert.rst

Combines pytest.mark.xfail with RaisesGroup to xfail only when the test fails with an ExceptionGroup containing IndexError. This is the recommended way to match exception groups.

```python
def f():
    raise ExceptionGroup("", [IndexError()])


@pytest.mark.xfail(raises=RaisesGroup(IndexError))
def test_f():
    f()
```

### Parametrize a test class with @pytest.mark.parametrize

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/parametrize.rst

Applies @pytest.mark.parametrize to a class, so every test method in the class runs with each parameter set. The class has two test methods, each executed twice with n=1 and n=3.

```python
import pytest


@pytest.mark.parametrize("n,expected", [(1, 2), (3, 4)])
class TestClass:
    def test_simple_case(self, n, expected):
        assert n + 1 == expected

    def test_weird_simple_case(self, n, expected):
        assert (n * 1) + 1 == expected
```

--------------------------------

### Replace get_marker with iter_markers for additive marks

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/historical-notes.rst

Use Node.iter_markers(name) when marks compose additively, such as skipif(condition) marks, where all conditions should be evaluated. Iterate over each mark and handle their args and kwargs individually.

```python
# replace this
skipif = item.get_marker("skipif")
if skipif:
    for condition in skipif.args:
        # eval condition
        ...

# by this:
for skipif in item.iter_markers("skipif"):
    condition = skipif.args[0]
    # eval condition
```

### Ignore specific deprecation warnings (TOML)

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/capture-warnings.rst

Use filterwarnings in TOML configuration to ignore a specific deprecation warning pattern, e.g., from third-party libraries.

```toml
[pytest]
filterwarnings = [
    'ignore:.*U.*mode is deprecated:DeprecationWarning',
]
```

### Assertions about expected exceptions

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/assert.rst

Use pytest.raises as a context manager to assert that a specific exception is raised.

```python
import pytest


def test_zero_division():
    with pytest.raises(ZeroDivisionError):
        1 / 0
```

--------------------------------

### Accessing captured output with capsys fixture

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/capture-stdout-stderr.rst

Test function using the capsys fixture to capture and assert stdout and stderr output. It reads captured output with readouterr(), checks the values, then prints more and reads again to verify the new output.

```python
def test_myoutput(capsys):  # or use "capfd" for fd-level
    print("hello")
    sys.stderr.write("world\n")
    captured = capsys.readouterr()
    assert captured.out == "hello\n"
    assert captured.err == "world\n"
    print("next")
    captured = capsys.readouterr()
    assert captured.out == "next\n"
```

### Disable capture with capsys.disabled()

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/capture-stdout-stderr.rst

Use capsys.disabled() as a context manager to temporarily disable capture within a test. Output printed inside the with block goes directly to sys.stdout and is not captured, while output outside is captured and available via readouterr().

```python
def test_disabling_capturing(capsys):
    print("this output is captured")
    with capsys.disabled():
        print("output not captured, going directly to sys.stdout")
    print("this output is also captured")
```

Command-line option to force running and reporting of xfail marked tests as if they were not marked, and also causes pytest.xfail to have no effect.

```bash

```

--------------------------------

### Yield-style test (removed)

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/deprecations.rst

Yield-style tests are no longer supported. This form of test function doesn't support fixtures properly and users should switch to pytest.mark.parametrize.

```python
def check(x, y):
    assert x**x == y


def test_squared():
    yield check, 2, 4
    yield check, 3, 9
```

--------------------------------

### Disable captured content reporting with --show-capture=no

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/logging.rst

Disables reporting of captured content (stdout, stderr, and logs) on failed tests completely.

```bash
pytest --show-capture=no
```

--------------------------------

### group_contains with depth parameter

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/assert.rst

Demonstrates the depth parameter of group_contains. By default it searches recursively; depth=1 matches exceptions directly in the top ExceptionGroup, depth=2 matches those in a nested group.

```python
def test_exception_in_group_at_given_depth():
    with pytest.raises(ExceptionGroup) as excinfo:
        raise ExceptionGroup(
            "Group message",
            [
                RuntimeError(),
                ExceptionGroup(
                    "Nested group",
                    [
                        TypeError(),
                    ],
                ),
            ],
        )
    assert excinfo.group_contains(RuntimeError, depth=1)
    assert excinfo.group_contains(TypeError, depth=2)
    assert not excinfo.group_contains(RuntimeError, depth=2)
    assert not excinfo.group_contains(TypeError, depth=1)
```

--------------------------------

### Deprecated and replacement for fixture registration with nodeid

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/deprecations.rst

Shows the deprecated use of nodeid strings in fixture registration APIs and the recommended node-based alternative. The deprecated calls use nodeid="tests/sub" while the replacement uses node=directory_node and pytest.register_fixture.

```python
# Deprecated
fixture_manager.parsefactories(plugin_obj, nodeid="tests/sub")
fixture_manager._register_fixture(name="fix", func=func, nodeid="tests/sub")

# Use instead
fixture_manager.parsefactories(holder=plugin_obj, node=directory_node)
pytest.register_fixture(name="fix", func=func, node=directory_node)
```

- **returncode** (int) - Optional - The return code to use. Defaults to None.

### caplog.records

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/logging.rst

Attribute that provides access to all `logging.LogRecord` instances captured during the test run. Useful for asserting on log message contents. Records are from the current test stage only.

```APIDOC
## caplog.records

### Description
Attribute containing all the `logging.LogRecord` instances sent to the logger during the test run. Useful for asserting on the contents of log messages. The records are from the current stage only (setup, call, or teardown).

### Example
```python
def test_baz(caplog):
    func_under_test()
    for record in caplog.records:
        assert record.levelname != "CRITICAL"
```

### Notes
- Contains records from the current stage only, so inside the `setup` phase it contains only setup logs, same with `call` and `teardown` phases.
```

### Using pytest.RaisesGroup for exception groups

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/assert.rst

Shows how to use pytest.RaisesGroup to assert that an exception group contains specific exception types. It accepts a match parameter for the group message and a check parameter for an arbitrary callable.

```python
def test_exception_in_group():
    with pytest.RaisesGroup(ValueError):
        raise ExceptionGroup("group msg", [ValueError("value msg")])
    with pytest.RaisesGroup(ValueError, TypeError):
        raise ExceptionGroup("msg", [ValueError("foo"), TypeError("bar")])
```

```python
def test_raisesgroup_match_and_check():
    with pytest.RaisesGroup(BaseException, match="my group msg"):
        raise BaseExceptionGroup("my group msg", [KeyboardInterrupt()])
    with pytest.RaisesGroup(
        Exception, check=lambda eg: isinstance(eg.__cause__, ValueError)
    ):
        raise ExceptionGroup("", [TypeError()]) from ValueError()
```

Sets the junit_logging option in a TOML config file to write captured stdout to the JUnit XML file. Valid values are "no", "log", "system-out", "system-err", "out-err", and "all".

```toml
[pytest]
junit_logging = "system-out"

### Use pytest fixture in unittest.TestCase subclass

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/unittest.rst

This test class uses the db_class fixture via the @pytest.mark.usefixtures decorator. The deliberately failing assert statements allow inspecting the self.db values in the traceback.

```python
# content of test_unittest_db.py

import unittest

import pytest


@pytest.mark.usefixtures("db_class")
class MyTest(unittest.TestCase):
    def test_method1(self):
        assert hasattr(self, "db")
        assert 0, self.db  # fail for demo purposes

    def test_method2(self):
        assert 0, self.db  # fail for demo purposes
```

Use the xfail marker to indicate that a test is expected to fail. The test will run, but no traceback will be reported when it fails; instead, it will be listed as XFAIL or XPASS in the terminal reporting.

```python
@pytest.mark.xfail
def test_function(): ...
```

### Override fixture with test parameter

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/fixtures.rst

Shows how a test parameter value overrides a fixture value, even if the test does not use the fixture directly in its function prototype.

```python
# content of tests/test_something.py
import pytest

@pytest.mark.parametrize('username', ['directly-overridden-username'])
def test_username(username):
    assert username == 'directly-overridden-username'

@pytest.mark.parametrize('username', ['directly-overridden-username-other'])
def test_username_other(other_username):
    assert other_username == 'other-directly-overridden-username-other'
```

--------------------------------

### Hide only specific exceptions with __tracebackhide__ callable

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/example/simple.rst

Set __tracebackhide__ to a callable that receives the ExceptionInfo object. Use operator.methodcaller to hide only ConfigException instances, leaving other exceptions visible.

```python
import operator

import pytest


class ConfigException(Exception):
    pass


def checkconfig(x):
    __tracebackhide__ = operator.methodcaller("errisinstance", ConfigException)
    if not hasattr(x, "config"):
        raise ConfigException(f"not configured: {x}")


def test_something():
    checkconfig(42)
```

[pytest]
strict_config = true

### Customize log level colors with add_color_level

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/logging.rst

Use pytest_configure hook to change the color of an existing log level and add a color to a custom log level. This feature is experimental and may change without notice.

```python
@pytest.hookimpl(trylast=True)
def pytest_configure(config):
    logging_plugin = config.pluginmanager.get_plugin("logging-plugin")

    # Change color on existing log level
    logging_plugin.log_cli_handler.formatter.add_color_level(logging.INFO, "cyan")

    # Add color to a custom log level (a custom log level `SPAM` is already set up)
    logging_plugin.log_cli_handler.formatter.add_color_level(logging.SPAM, "blue")
```

### Set PYTEST_PLUGINS environment variable

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/reference/reference.rst

Loads plugins from a comma-separated list of modules or plugin entry point names. This environment variable is used to specify plugins explicitly.

```bash
export PYTEST_PLUGINS=mymodule.plugin,xdist
```

### Fix sync test depending on async fixture

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/deprecations.rst

Shows the problematic pattern of a synchronous test depending on an async fixture, and the recommended fix: wrap the async fixture in a synchronous fixture that returns the coroutine. The first snippet triggers an 'unawaited coroutine' warning; the second suppresses it by returning the coroutine from a sync fixture.

```python
import asyncio
import pytest


@pytest.fixture
async def unawaited_fixture():
    return 1


def test_foo(unawaited_fixture):
    assert 1 == asyncio.run(unawaited_fixture)
```

```python
import asyncio
import pytest


@pytest.fixture
def unawaited_fixture():
    async def inner_fixture():
        return 1

    return inner_fixture()
```

>>> import pytest

--------------------------------

### Session-scoped fixture to call callme on all test classes

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/example/special.rst

Defines a session-scoped autouse fixture that walks all collected test items and calls the callme method on each test class that defines it. The fixture uses request.node to access the session and item.getparent(pytest.Class) to find the class.

```python
# content of conftest.py

import pytest


@pytest.fixture(scope="session", autouse=True)
def callattr_ahead_of_alltests(request):
    print("callattr_ahead_of_alltests called")
    seen = {None}
    session = request.node
    for item in session.items:
        cls = item.getparent(pytest.Class)
        if cls not in seen:
            if hasattr(cls.obj, "callme"):
                cls.obj.callme()
            seen.add(cls)
```

### Test passing assertion with pytester

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/CONTRIBUTING.rst

Use this test to verify that a test with a passing assertion results in zero failures and one pass. It creates a temporary test file with a simple assert True and runs pytest, then checks the outcome counts.

```python
def test_true_assertion(pytester):
    pytester.makepyfile(
        """
        def test_foo():
            assert True
    """
    )
    result = pytester.runpytest()
    result.assert_outcomes(failed=0, passed=1)
```

--------------------------------

### recwarn fixture

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/capture-warnings.rst

Fixture that records warnings raised during a test function and provides access to them.

```APIDOC
## recwarn fixture

### Description
Fixture that records warnings raised during a test function. It automatically resets the warnings filter at the end of the test, so no global state is leaked.

### Usage
Declare `recwarn` as a parameter in the test function to access the recorded warnings.

### Methods
- **pop(warning_class)** - Removes and returns the first warning of the given class.
- **len(recwarn)** - Returns the number of recorded warnings.

### Attributes of WarningMessage objects
- **message** - The warning message.
- **category** - The warning category.
- **filename** - The file where the warning was issued.
- **lineno** - The line number where the warning was issued.

### Example
```python
import warnings

def test_hello(recwarn):
    warnings.warn("hello", UserWarning)
    assert len(recwarn) == 1
    w = recwarn.pop(UserWarning)
    assert issubclass(w.category, UserWarning)
    assert str(w.message) == "hello"
    assert w.filename
    assert w.lineno
```
```

--------------------------------

### Global patch to prevent requests from performing HTTP operations

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/monkeypatch.rst

An autouse fixture that deletes requests.sessions.Session.request for every test, preventing any HTTP requests from being made. Place this in conftest.py to apply it globally.

```python
# contents of conftest.py
import pytest


@pytest.fixture(autouse=True)
def no_requests(monkeypatch):
    """Remove requests.sessions.Session.request for all tests."""
    monkeypatch.delattr("requests.sessions.Session.request")
```

### group_contains with match parameter

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/assert.rst

Uses excinfo.group_contains() to test for an exception inside an ExceptionGroup. The match keyword works like pytest.raises, and the assertion also verifies that a TypeError is not present.

```python
def test_exception_in_group():
    with pytest.raises(ExceptionGroup) as excinfo:
        raise ExceptionGroup(
            "Group message",
            [
                RuntimeError("Exception 123 raised"),
            ],
        )
    assert excinfo.group_contains(RuntimeError, match=r".* 123 .*")
    assert not excinfo.group_contains(TypeError)
```

--------------------------------

### Skip a doctest check with doctest: +SKIP

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/doctest.rst

Skip a single check inside a doctest using the standard doctest.SKIP directive. The first check is skipped, but the second check still runs.

```python
def test_random(y):
    """
    >>> random.random()  # doctest: +SKIP
    0.156231223

    >>> 1 + 1
    2
    """
```

### Pass multiple filters to a single filterwarnings mark

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/capture-warnings.rst

Later arguments take precedence, matching warnings.filterwarnings behavior. This is the reverse of the decorator ordering approach.

```python
# Later arguments take precedence, matching warnings.filterwarnings behavior.
@pytest.mark.filterwarnings("error", "ignore:api v1")
def test_one():
    assert api_v1() == 1
```

### Overwriting a parametrized fixture while reusing the super fixture value

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/changelog.rst

Shows the fix for issue 1953: a parametrized fixture in conftest.py is overwritten in test_foo.py while reusing the super fixture value. The test file's fixture takes the original fixture as an argument and multiplies it by 2.

```python
# conftest.py
import pytest


@pytest.fixture(params=[1, 2])
def foo(request):
    return request.param

```

```python
# test_foo.py
import pytest


@pytest.fixture
def foo(foo):
    return foo * 2

```

### Apply markers to individual parametrized tests

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/example/markers.rst

Markers can be applied to specific instances within a parametrize call using pytest.param.

```python
import pytest


@pytest.mark.foo
@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    ("n", "expected"), [(1, 2), pytest.param(1, 3, marks=pytest.mark.bar), (2, 3)]
)
def test_increment(n, expected):
    assert n + 1 == expected
```

### Disable assertion rewriting cache with sys.dont_write_bytecode

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/assert.rst

Place at the top of conftest.py to disable caching of rewritten assertion modules to disk. This avoids leaving stale .pyc files in projects that move files around a lot, while still retaining assertion introspection benefits.

```python
import sys

sys.dont_write_bytecode = True
```

### Configure log_cli_level in pyproject.toml

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/reference/reference.rst

Sets the log_cli_level option in a TOML config file to set the minimum log level for live logging. Accepts level names or integer values.

```toml
[pytest]
log_cli_level = "INFO"
log_cli_level = 10
```

--------------------------------

### pytest.mark (Custom Marks)

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/reference/reference.rst

Marks are created dynamically using the factory object pytest.mark and applied as a decorator. This allows attaching custom metadata to test functions, accessible via Node.iter_markers.

```APIDOC
## pytest.mark (Custom Marks)

### Description
Marks are created dynamically using the factory object pytest.mark and applied as a decorator. They attach a Mark object to the collected Item, accessible via Node.iter_markers.

### Usage
Apply as a decorator with optional arguments and keyword arguments.

### Example
```python
@pytest.mark.timeout(10, "slow", method="thread")
def test_function(): ...
```

### Attributes
- **mark.args** - Tuple of positional arguments passed to the mark.
- **mark.kwargs** - Dictionary of keyword arguments passed to the mark.

### Notes
When using multiple markers, the marker closest to the function is iterated over first.
```

--------------------------------

### Send test failures to pastebin

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/output.rst

Runs pytest with --pastebin=failed to submit test run information to a remote paste service and provide a URL for each failure. You can select tests as usual or add options like -x to send only one particular failure.

```bash
pytest --pastebin=failed
```

### pytest.warns(expected_warning: Exception, [match])

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/reference/reference.rst

Assert that a code block raises a specific warning. Optionally match the warning message.

```APIDOC
## pytest.warns(expected_warning: Exception, [match])

### Description
Assert that a code block raises a specific warning. Optionally match the warning message.

### Parameters
- **expected_warning** (Exception) - Required - The expected warning type.
- **match** (str) - Optional - A regex to match against the warning message.
```

--------------------------------

### strict_xfail configuration in TOML

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/reference/reference.rst

When true, tests marked with @pytest.mark.xfail that actually succeed will by default fail the test suite. Can also be enabled via the strict option. Renamed from xfail_strict in version 9.0; xfail_strict is accepted as an alias.

```toml
[pytest]
strict_xfail = true
```

### Disable a specific strictness option in pyproject.toml

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/explanation/goodpractices.rst

Enable strict mode but turn off a specific option (strict_parametrization_ids) in a TOML configuration file when having trouble with that option.

```toml

strict = true
strict_parametrization_ids = false
```

```APIDOC
## pytest.skip(reason, [allow_module_level=False])

### Description
Skip a test with the given reason. Optionally allow skipping at module level.

### Parameters
- **reason** (str) - Required - The skip message.
- **allow_module_level** (bool) - Optional - If True, allows skipping at module level. Defaults to False.

### Exceptions

### Skip all tests in a module based on a condition

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/skipping.rst

Set the pytestmark variable with pytest.mark.skipif to skip all tests in the module when the condition is true. The reason must be provided.

```python
pytestmark = pytest.mark.skipif(sys.platform == "win32", reason="tests for linux only")
```

--------------------------------

### Accessing mark attributes

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/reference/reference.rst

Shows the attributes of the mark object created by the custom mark. The args and kwargs attributes hold the positional and keyword arguments passed to the mark.

```python
mark.args == (10, "slow")
mark.kwargs == {"method": "thread"}
```

--------------------------------

### Session-scoped smtp_connection fixture

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/fixtures.rst

Declares a session-scoped smtp_connection fixture. The returned fixture value is shared for all tests requesting it across the entire test session.

```python
@pytest.fixture(scope="session")
def smtp_connection():
    # the returned fixture value will be shared for
    # all tests requesting it
    ...

```

--------------------------------

### strict configuration in TOML

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/reference/reference.rst

Enables strict mode, which turns on strict_config, strict_markers, strict_parametrization_ids, and strict_xfail. Explicitly setting an individual strictness option takes precedence over strict. Only enable if using a pinned/locked pytest version or if you want to adopt new strictness options.

```toml
[pytest]
strict = true
```

--------------------------------

### Assert text not in output with in operator

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/changelog.rst

Previous idiom for asserting that captured text does not contain a substring, using the in operator. The new LineMatcher.no_fnmatch_line and no_re_match_line functions produce better output on failure.

```python
result = testdir.runpytest()
assert text in result.stdout.str()
```

### Set log level with caplog.set_level

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/logging.rst

Changes the log level for captured log messages inside a test using the caplog fixture. By default the level is set on the root logger.

```python
def test_foo(caplog):
    caplog.set_level(logging.INFO)
```

### Legacy pytest.raises with callable and arguments

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/assert.rst

Legacy form of pytest.raises that takes a callable and its arguments. It executes func(x=-1) and asserts that ValueError is raised. This form predates the with statement and is rarely used now.

```python
def func(x):
    if x <= 0:
        raise ValueError("x needs to be larger than zero")


pytest.raises(ValueError, func, x=-1)
```

### record_property

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/builtin.rst

Fixture that adds extra properties to the test report.

```APIDOC
## Fixture: record_property

### Description
Add extra properties to the calling test. User properties become part of the test report and are available to configured reporters, like JUnit XML.

### Usage
The fixture is callable with name, value. The value is automatically XML-encoded.

### Example
```python
def test_function(record_property):
    record_property("example_key", 1)
```
```

### capsys

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/reference/reference.rst

Fixture that captures output sent to stdout and stderr. It provides access to the captured output via the returned CaptureFixture instance.

```APIDOC
## capsys

### Description
Fixture that captures output sent to stdout and stderr. It provides access to the captured output via the returned CaptureFixture instance.

### Usage
Declare as an argument to a test function to capture output.

### Example
```python
def test_output(capsys):
    print("hello")
    out, err = capsys.readouterr()
    assert out == "hello\n"
```
```

--------------------------------

### Add colors to custom log levels using add_color_level

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/changelog.rst

Use this snippet in a pytest_configure hook to add colors to custom log levels on the CLI log. It retrieves the logging plugin and calls add_color_level on the log CLI handler's formatter, specifying the log level and color.

```python
logging_plugin = config.pluginmanager.get_plugin('logging-plugin')
logging_plugin.log_cli_handler.formatter.add_color_level(logging.INFO, 'cyan')
logging_plugin.log_cli_handler.formatter.add_color_level(logging.SPAM, 'blue')
```

### Deprecation warning: Node internal class access

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/deprecations.rst

Warning message issued when accessing Module, Function, Class, Instance, File, or Item through Node instances. Users should import pytest and access these objects via the pytest module.

```text
usage of Function.Module is deprecated, please use pytest.Module instead
```

--------------------------------

### Custom failure message when no warning with pytest.warns

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/capture-warnings.rst

Use this to produce a custom failure message when no warning is issued. If f() does not emit a warning, the test fails with the custom message.

```python
def test():
    with pytest.warns(Warning) as record:
        f()
        if not record:
            pytest.fail("Expected a warning!")
```

--------------------------------

### Enforce strict markers in pytest.ini

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/mark.rst

Set strict_markers to true and define markers in INI to raise errors on unknown marks.

```ini
[pytest]
strict_markers = true
markers =
    slow: marks tests as slow (deselect with '-m "not slow"')
    serial
```

### Apply multiple marks with pytestmark

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/reference/reference.rst

Applies a list of marks to all test functions and methods in a test module. Marks are applied in left-to-right order.

```python
import pytest

pytestmark = [pytest.mark.integration, pytest.mark.slow]
```

### Skipping with config.getvalue in string condition

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/historical-notes.rst

String condition that uses the pytest config object to skip based on a test configuration value. The config object is part of the namespace used for evaluating condition strings.

```python
@pytest.mark.skipif("not config.getvalue('db')")
def test_function(): ...
```

### New collection tree after pytest 8 changes

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/deprecations.rst

New collection tree as shown by `pytest --collect-only top/` with the Session node added for clarity.

```text
<Session>
  <Dir myroot>
    <Dir top>
      <Dir aaa>
        <Module test_aaa.py>
          <Function test_it>
      <Module test_a.py>
        <Function test_it>
      <Package test_b>
        <Module test_b.py>
          <Function test_it>
      <Module test_c.py>
        <Function test_it>
      <Package zzz>
        <Module test_zzz.py>
          <Function test_it>
```

### Old node id with ::() notation

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/changelog.rst

Old node id format that used ::() to denote a test class instance. This notation has been removed in pytest 4.0.

```text
test_foo.py::Test::()::test_bar
```

### Detect if running from within a pytest run

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/example/simple.rst

Check the PYTEST_VERSION environment variable to determine if the code is being called by pytest. This is generally discouraged but can be used when absolutely necessary.

```python
import os


if os.environ.get("PYTEST_VERSION") is not None:
    # Things you want to do if your code is called by pytest.
    ...
else:
    # Things you want to do if your code is not called by pytest.
    ...
```

--------------------------------

### Add property via custom marker in conftest.py

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/output.rst

Defines a pytest_collection_modifyitems hook in conftest.py that reads markers named 'test_id' and appends their values as user properties to test items.

```python
# content of conftest.py


def pytest_collection_modifyitems(session, config, items):
    for item in items:
        for marker in item.iter_markers(name="test_id"):
            test_id = marker.args[0]
            item.user_properties.append(("test_id", test_id))
```

### Incorrect mock assertion with assert

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/changelog.rst

Common mistake: wrapping a mock assertion in an assert statement. This triggers a warning because the mock method already performs the assertion.

```python
assert mocked_object.assert_called_with(3, 4, 5, key="value")
```

--------------------------------

### Retrieve data from item.stash during pytest_runtest_teardown

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/writing_hook_functions.rst

Retrieves and modifies stashed data during the teardown phase, printing a message if the 'been_there' flag is not set.

```python
def pytest_runtest_teardown(item: pytest.Item) -> None:
    if not item.stash[been_there_key]:
        print("Oh?")
    item.stash[done_that_key] = "yes!"
```

>   assert 1 in [0, 2, 3, 4, 5]

### Apply multiple warning filters with decorators

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/capture-warnings.rst

Use @pytest.mark.filterwarnings to ignore 'api v1' warnings and turn all others into errors. Decorators are evaluated in reverse order, so earlier decorators take precedence.

```python
# Ignore "api v1" warnings, but fail on all other warnings
@pytest.mark.filterwarnings("ignore:api v1")
@pytest.mark.filterwarnings("error")
def test_one():
    assert api_v1() == 1
```

--------------------------------

### Ignore deprecation warnings with filterwarnings

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/capture-warnings.rst

Configure pytest to ignore DeprecationWarning messages matching the regular expression ".*U.*mode is deprecated".

```ini
[pytest]
filterwarnings =
    ignore:.*U.*mode is deprecated:DeprecationWarning
```

```APIDOC
## caplog.record_tuples

### Description
Attribute containing a list of tuples `(logger_name, level, message)` for all captured log records. Useful for asserting that certain messages have been logged under a given logger name with a given severity and message.

### Example
```python
def test_foo(caplog):
    logging.getLogger().info("boo %s", "arg")
    assert caplog.record_tuples == [("root", logging.INFO, "boo arg")]
```
```

def pytest_addhooks(pluginmanager):

    from my_app.tests import sample_hook

    pluginmanager.add_hookspecs(sample_hook)

### Apply indirect on particular arguments

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/example/parametrize.rst

Shows how to apply indirect only to specific arguments by passing a list of argument names. Here, only 'x' is indirect, while 'y' receives the value directly.

```python
# content of test_indirect_list.py

import pytest


@pytest.fixture(scope="function")
def x(request):
    return request.param * 3


@pytest.fixture(scope="function")
def y(request):
    return request.param * 2


@pytest.mark.parametrize("x, y", [("a", "b")], indirect=["x"])
def test_indirect(x, y):
    assert x == "aaa"
    assert y == "b"
```

### Jenkins XSD schema for testcase validation

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/output.rst

XML Schema Definition (XSD) used by Jenkins to validate testcase elements in JUnit XML reports. Shows allowed attributes and child elements.

```xml
<xs:element name="testcase">
    <xs:complexType>
        <xs:sequence>
            <xs:element ref="skipped" minOccurs="0" maxOccurs="1"/>
            <xs:element ref="error" minOccurs="0" maxOccurs="unbounded"/>
            <xs:element ref="failure" minOccurs="0" maxOccurs="unbounded"/>
            <xs:element ref="system-out" minOccurs="0" maxOccurs="unbounded"/>
            <xs:element ref="system-err" minOccurs="0" maxOccurs="unbounded"/>
        </xs:sequence>
        <xs:attribute name="name" type="xs:string" use="required"/>
        <xs:attribute name="assertions" type="xs:string" use="optional"/>
        <xs:attribute name="time" type="xs:string" use="optional"/>
        <xs:attribute name="classname" type="xs:string" use="optional"/>
        <xs:attribute name="status" type="xs:string" use="optional"/>
    </xs:complexType>
</xs:element>
```

### pytest.deprecated_call

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/capture-warnings.rst

Context manager to assert that a function call triggers a deprecation warning.

```APIDOC
## pytest.deprecated_call

### Description
Checks that a certain function call triggers a `DeprecationWarning`, `PendingDeprecationWarning`, or `FutureWarning`. The test will fail if the expected warning is not issued.

### Usage
Use as a context manager around the code that should trigger the warning.

### Parameters
- **expected_warning** (type) - Optional - The expected warning class. Defaults to `DeprecationWarning`.

### Example
```python
import pytest

def test_myfunction_deprecated():
    with pytest.deprecated_call():
        myfunction(17)
```

### Behavior
- Fails if the wrapped code does not issue a deprecation warning.
```

### Fixture error stops execution with autouse fixture

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/explanation/fixtures.rst

Shows a chain of fixtures where an autouse fixture appends to a shared order list. If an earlier fixture raises an exception, pytest stops running subsequent fixtures and the test is marked as an error, not a failure.

```python
import pytest


@pytest.fixture
def order():
    return []


@pytest.fixture
def append_first(order):
    order.append(1)


@pytest.fixture
def append_second(order, append_first):
    order.extend([2])


@pytest.fixture(autouse=True)
def append_third(order, append_second):
    order += [3]


def test_order(order):
    assert order == [1, 2, 3]
```

--------------------------------

### Assertions about approximate equality

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/assert.rst

Use pytest.approx to compare floating-point numbers or arrays, avoiding issues with small rounding errors.

```python
import pytest
import numpy as np


def test_floats():
    assert (0.1 + 0.2) == pytest.approx(0.3)


def test_arrays():
    a = np.array([1.0, 2.0, 3.0])
    b = np.array([0.9999, 2.0001, 3.0])
    assert a == pytest.approx(b)
```

```python
def test_foo(record_testsuite_property):
    record_testsuite_property("ARCH", "PPC")
    record_testsuite_property("STORAGE_TYPE", "CEPH")

### pytest.mark.xfail

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/reference/reference.rst

Marks a test function as expected to fail. This decorator can be used to indicate that a test is expected to fail under certain conditions, with options to control execution and strictness.

```APIDOC
## pytest.mark.xfail

### Description
Marks a test function as *expected to fail*.

### Method
Decorator

### Parameters
#### Keyword Arguments
- **condition** (Union[bool, str]) - Optional - Condition for marking the test function as xfail (True/False or a condition string). If a bool, you also have to specify reason.
- **reason** (str) - Optional - Reason why the test function is marked as xfail.
- **raises** (Type[Exception] | Tuple[Type[Exception], ...] | None) - Optional - Exception class (or tuple of classes) expected to be raised by the test function; other exceptions will fail the test. Subclasses of the classes passed will also result in a match.
- **run** (bool) - Optional - Whether the test function should actually be executed. If False, the function will always xfail and will not be executed.
- **strict** (bool) - Optional - If False, the function will be shown as xfailed if it fails and as xpass if it passes, without failing the test suite. If True, an unexpected pass will fail the test suite. Defaults to strict_xfail, which is False by default.

### Example
```python
@pytest.mark.xfail(reason="known bug")
def test_function():
    ...
```
```

## pytest.mark.skipif(condition, *, reason=None)

### Add choices validation to --cmdopt option

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/example/simple.rst

This conftest.py adds a choices parameter to the --cmdopt option, restricting valid values to type1 and type2. Invalid choices will cause pytest to error with a usage message.

```python
# content of conftest.py
import pytest


def pytest_addoption(parser):
    parser.addoption(
        "--cmdopt",
        action="store",
        default="type1",
        help="my option: type1 or type2",
        choices=("type1", "type2"),
    )
```

--------------------------------

### Deprecated: pytest_namespace hook usage

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/deprecations.rst

Deprecated usage of the pytest_namespace hook to inject symbols into pytest's namespace. Plugin authors should instead require users to import plugin modules directly.

```python
class MySymbol: ...


    def pytest_namespace():
        return {"my_symbol": MySymbol()}
```

### caplog.set_level(level, logger=None)

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/logging.rst

Sets the log level for captured log messages during a test. By default, it affects the root logger, but a specific logger can be targeted. The level is restored automatically after the test.

```APIDOC
## caplog.set_level(level, logger=None)

### Description
Sets the log level for the captured log messages. By default, the level is set on the root logger, but a specific logger can be targeted using the `logger` parameter. The log level is restored automatically at the end of the test.

### Parameters
- **level** (int) - Required - The logging level to set (e.g., `logging.INFO`).
- **logger** (str) - Optional - The name of the logger to set the level on. If not provided, the root logger is used.

### Example
```python
def test_foo(caplog):
    caplog.set_level(logging.INFO)
```

### Notes
- The log level is restored automatically at the end of the test.
- The `logger` parameter allows setting the level on any logger, e.g., `caplog.set_level(logging.CRITICAL, logger="root.baz")`.
```

--------------------------------

### Set log level for a specific logger with caplog.set_level

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/logging.rst

Sets the log level of any logger by passing the logger name to caplog.set_level. The log levels set are restored automatically at the end of the test.

```python
def test_foo(caplog):
    caplog.set_level(logging.CRITICAL, logger="root.baz")
```

### Apply a single mark with pytestmark

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/reference/reference.rst

Applies a single mark to all test functions and methods in a test module. Declared at the global level.

```python
import pytest

pytestmark = pytest.mark.webtest
```

### fixture: monkeypatch

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/reference/reference.rst

The monkeypatch fixture provides a MonkeyPatch instance for safely patching attributes, dictionary items, environment variables, and more during tests. It automatically undoes changes after the test.

```APIDOC
## Fixture: monkeypatch

### Description
Provides a MonkeyPatch instance for safely patching attributes, dictionary items, environment variables, and more during tests.

### Usage
Declare as a fixture parameter in a test function.

### Tutorial
See :ref:`monkeypatching`

### Returns
- `pytest.MonkeyPatch` instance
```

### Configure log_cli_format in pytest.ini

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/reference/reference.rst

Sets the log_cli_format option in an INI config file to a logging-compatible format string for live logging messages.

```ini
[pytest]
log_cli_format = %(asctime)s %(levelname)s %(message)s
```

### Set log_file_mode in INI

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/reference/reference.rst

Sets the mode for opening the logging file. Use "a" to append or "w" to recreate.

```ini
[pytest]
log_file_mode = a
```

--------------------------------

### caplog.clear()

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/how-to/logging.rst

Resets the captured log records in a test. Useful for clearing logs between different parts of a test.

```APIDOC
## caplog.clear()

### Description
Resets the captured log records in a test. Useful for clearing logs between different parts of a test.

### Example
```python
def test_something_with_clearing_records(caplog):
    some_method_that_creates_log_records()
    caplog.clear()
    your_test_method()
    assert ["Foo"] == [rec.message for rec in caplog.records]
```
```

--------------------------------

### Set log_file_format in TOML

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/reference/reference.rst

Sets a logging-compatible string used to format logging messages redirected to the logging file.

```toml
[pytest]
log_file_format = "%(asctime)s %(levelname)s %(message)s"
```

### capfd

Source: https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/blob/main/doc/en/reference/reference.rst

Fixture that captures output sent to file descriptors 1 and 2. It provides access to the captured output via the returned CaptureFixture instance.

```APIDOC
## capfd

### Description
Fixture that captures output sent to file descriptors 1 and 2. It provides access to the captured output via the returned CaptureFixture instance.

### Usage
Declare as an argument to a test function to capture output.

### Example
```python
def test_output(capfd):
    print("hello")
    out, err = capfd.readouterr()
    assert out == "hello\n"
```
```
